# Thus ends the First Chapter of the Natal Horoscope.
## Now, the Multipliers for the Signs and Planets

Taurus and Leo are to be multiplied by ten; Gemini and Scorpio by eight <note>original: "vasubhih," a word-numeral for 8</note>; Libra and Sagittarius by seven; Virgo and Capricorn by five; and the others <note>Aries and Pisces</note> are multiplied by the same value as Aries <note>typically 7 or 10 depending on the system</note>. These are the sign-multipliers in order. For Jupiter, the Sun, Venus, and the Moon, the multipliers are ten, eight, seven, and five respectively. 

The multipliers for Mercury and the remaining planets <note>Mars and Saturn</note> are thus stated by Atreya <note>a celebrated sage and medical/astrological authority</note>. 

May the illustrious Shiva be victorious! In the city of Nandidwarapura <note>likely a historical city in the Kannauj region</note>, there was a man from the land of Kanyakubja <note>modern-day Kannauj, Uttar Pradesh</note>, whose son was Shivadasa. His son is King Shambhu, a devoted practitioner who is wise in charity, the service of the twice-born <note>term: Brahmins</note>, and ritual purification. Like a modern-day Karna <note>a hero from the Mahabharata known for his peerless generosity</note>, he gladdens the hearts of the learned and stands victorious with great splendor. 

Having obtained his position from the illustrious Sovereign of the World <note>likely a reference to the Mughal Emperor or a high regional overlord</note>, the wise King Shambhu Rama is devoted to Shiva. He delights in music, dancing, and various amusements; he possesses a keen intellect for the scriptures and is exceedingly generous. 

His time of birth is written here for the purpose of establishing the results of his personal birth-chart. It occurred when the **Shalivahana Saka** era <note>a standard historical calendar in India</note> had passed through one thousand, five hundred, and eighty-four years <note>Saka 1584, corresponding to 1662 CE</note>. 

In the **Vikrama Samvat** year 1715, during the auspicious bright fortnight of the month of Jyeshtha <note>May–June</note>, on the thirteenth lunar day <note>term: Tithi</note>—the day of the God of Love—on a Wednesday. 

On that day, the lunar date reached 14 <term>Ghatis</term> and 15 <term>Palas</term> <note>Traditional units of time: 1 Ghati is 24 minutes; 1 Pala is 24 seconds</note>. The Moon was in the constellation of Krittika <note>the Pleiades</note> for 12 Ghatis and 48 Palas. The astrological "Yoga" was Dhriti (21 Ghatis, 15 Palas) and the "Karana" was Vanija. 

Thus, according to the Five Limbs of the Calendar <term>Panchanga</term>, after sunrise on that day, at 9 Ghatis and 11 Palas, with a day-length of 33 Ghatis and 54 Palas, the birth of the virtuous King Shambhu Rama occurred. At that moment, the "elapsed time" was 9;18 and the "remaining time" was 14;42. 

**Here is the example calculation:**
Year: Samvat 1715, Saka 1584. Month: Jyeshtha. Fortnight: Bright. Day: 13, Wednesday. 
Time: 14 Ghatis, 15 Palas. Nakshatra: Krittika (12 Ghatis, 48 Palas). Yoga: Dhriti (21 Ghatis, 15 Palas). Karana: Vanija.

Regarding the examination of the strength of the Ascendant <note>term: Lagna; the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of birth</note> and the seven planets, one should apply the multipliers of 9, 4, and 8. ### The Method for Writing the Eight-Fold Grid
The following is the sequence for writing the *Ashtakavarga* <note>term: Ashtakavarga; a system of "eight-fold" grids where planets contribute points to signs to determine their strength</note> of the Sun and the other planets as previously described. It is said that the counts of 14, 7, and 11 are determined there. Among those, in the specific divisions, if the points exceed 21 in their own grid, then in that planetary period <note>original: "dasha"</note>, through effort, one overcomes enemies. Through the grace of the Guru <note>the planet Jupiter or a spiritual teacher</note>, he will obtain sons and a wife—of this there is no doubt. 

#### The Results of the Points
*   **One, two, or three points:** Results in the loss of success or destruction of wealth.
*   **Four points:** These are called "medium" results. 
*   **Five and above:** Results in the arising of virtues and growth.
*   **Eight points:** This brings the perfection of all goals <note>original: "sarva-siddhida"</note>.

One should calculate the daily points for all the planets. When the total is above 28 points, the results are auspicious; when below, they are inauspicious. Thus, the daily results are described in the <term>Jatakabharana</term> <note>a famous 15th-century astrological compendium by Dhuṇḍhirāja</note>. By totaling the planetary lines <note>original: "khetarekha"</note>, whether the sum is high or low determines the good or bad results.

### The Rules for Trinal Reduction
I have composed these verses regarding the method for <term>Trinal Reduction</term> <note>original: "Trikona Shodhana"; the process of simplifying points within groups of signs belonging to the same element, such as the Fire signs Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius</note>. 

Among the signs of a "triangle" <note>trine</note>, find the house with the least number of points. Subtract that minimum value from the points in all three signs. If one of those signs has zero points <note>original: "gagana," meaning sky or zero</note>, then no reduction should be performed in that trine at all. If the points in all three signs are equal, then all points should be removed <note>set to zero</note>. This is the established method for the Trinal Reduction.

### The Reduction for Dual Lordship
Next, a special rule regarding <term>Dual Lordship</term> <note>original: "Ekadhipatya"; refers to planets like Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn, which each rule two different zodiac signs</note>. 

If both signs ruled by the same planet are occupied by other planets, no reduction is made. If one sign is occupied and the other is empty, the empty sign's points are reduced based on the occupied sign. If one has fewer points than the other, subtract the smaller value from the larger. If one sign is empty of points, the reduction is handled according to the specific planetary strengths...

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the Ashtakavarga system of astrology. Having established the initial point counts on the previous page, the text now describes the two-step "reduction" process (shodhana) used to refine these points before calculating the subject's lifespan (Ayu).</meta>

If the results are equal between two signs, they remain as they are. **20** After the initial counts are placed in the grid, one must calculate the "reduction" or refinement of these values. First, the **Trine Reduction** <note>term: Trikona Shodhana; the process of balancing points between the four groups of three signs that share the same element</note> must be performed, and afterwards, the **Single Lordship Reduction** <note>term: Ekadhipatya Shodhana; a further refinement based on the fact that most planets rule two different zodiac signs</note>. **21**

### The Method of Trine Reduction
Now, the method for Trine Reduction and Single Lordship Reduction is explained. Within the wheel of the zodiac <note>term: Rashichakra; the circular map of the twelve signs</note>, one should arrange the houses to perform the trine refinement for all signs. **22** 

How are these trines defined? They are:
*   **Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius** <note>original: "Mesha-Simha-Haya"; Haya literally means 'horse,' referring to the Archer, Sagittarius</note>
*   **Taurus, Virgo, and Capricorn** <note>original: "Vrisha-Kanya-Mriga"; Mriga refers to the 'deer' or 'sea-monster' Capricorn</note>
*   **Libra, Aquarius, and Gemini** 
*   **Cancer, Scorpio, and Pisces** **23**

These groups of three are considered mutually related. Among the signs in a single trine, one should find the sign with the lowest number of points and subtract that value from the points in the other two signs of that trine. **24** If one house in the trine has no points, no reduction is made for that entire trine. A wise practitioner should understand this total refinement across all houses. **25**

### The Method of Single Lordship Reduction
After the trine reduction is complete, the wise scholar should perform the refinement for **Single Lordship**. Where a planet rules two signs, one must compare the remaining points of those two signs. **26** 

1. If one of the signs is occupied by a planet and the other is not, the reduction is handled differently. 
2. If the sign without a planet has fewer points, reduce it to zero. 
3. If the sign without a planet has more points, reduce its points to match the sign that contains a planet. **27**
4. If one sign is empty of planets and has more points than the other empty sign, reduce its points. If they are equal and both are empty, remove all points from both. **28** 
5. If both signs are occupied by planets, no reduction is ever to be made. If both are empty and have equal points, both should be cleared of points entirely. **29**

The Sun and the Moon <note>original: "Kulira-Simha"; referring to Cancer (the Crab) and Leo (the Lion)</note> are treated separately because they each rule only one sign; their results are considered unique. **30**

### Calculating the Final Multipliers and Longevity
Once the reduction is complete, the remaining points must be multiplied by the "Sign Multiplier" <note>term: Rashimana; a specific numerical value assigned to each zodiac sign</note>. If a planet occupies the sign, that value is then multiplied again by the "Planetary Multiplier." **31** 

For example, **Taurus** and **Leo** are both multiplied by 10. **32** One should multiply the points by the specific factors for the signs and the planets separately. If planets are present in the house, the results are treated with these specific numerical values. **33**

After multiplying, the total sum should be added together and then multiplied by 7. This total is then divided by 27 <note>representing the 27 Lunar Mansions or Nakshatras</note>. The resulting number provides the **years of life**. **34** The remainders of this division provide the months and days <note>original: "ghatika"; a traditional unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note>. This is how the span of **twenty-seven years** <note>original: "saptavimshati-varshani"</note> or the total longevity is declared. **35**

## The Practical Example

Now an example is written. First, the method for the **Reduction of the Trines**: <note>term: Trikona Shodhana; a mathematical step where points are removed from zodiac signs belonging to the same element (Fire, Earth, Air, Water)</note>

In the trines, one should subtract the lowest value from the three signs. This means in the three positions that form a trinal group, whatever the smallest value is, that amount is subtracted from all three positions.

For example, in the **Eight-fold Table of the Sun**, the points recorded starting from Aries are:

| Sign | Original Points | Calculation | Resulting Points |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Aries** | 5 | 5 - 3 | 2 |
| **Leo** | 4 | 4 - 3 | 1 |
| **Sagittarius** | 3 | 3 - 3 | 0 |

<note>original: "Mesha 5, Simha 4, Dhanu 3. The lowest (3) is subtracted from the three."</note>

Similarly for the next group:
*   **Taurus**: 4
*   **Virgo**: 4
*   **Aquarius**: 5 <note>The text lists "Kumbha" (Aquarius), though standard systems usually group Taurus and Virgo with Capricorn (Makara).</note>

In these three, the lowest value is 4. After subtracting this from the three, the results are:
*   **Taurus**: 0
*   **Virgo**: 0
*   **Aquarius**: 1

Then for the next group:
*   **Gemini**: 5
*   **Libra**: 4
*   **[Missing/Unclear]**: [Value]

In these three, after subtracting... <note>The text breaks off mid-calculation for the third trinal group.</note>

# Page 28

<margin>Male 28</margin> For Libra: 1, for Aquarius: 1, and for Pisces: 6, Cancer: 3, Scorpio: 12. After the three are reduced <note>term: Shodhana; the process of "cleansing" or reducing the points in an astrological chart to find the net strength</note>, the results are: Cancer: 0, Scorpio: 0, Pisces: 0. 3 <margin>7</margin>
This is the first method. If there is a zero <note>term: Shunya; an empty house with no auspicious points</note> in a house within that trinal group <note>term: Trikona; a group of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element, such as Fire (Aries, Leo, Sagittarius)</note>, then one should not perform the reduction for that trine. Where a zero exists in one of the three trinal positions, the reduction is not to be done; instead, the result should be written exactly as it stands in the three positions. For example, in the Eight-fold Matrix of Mars <note>term: Bhaumashtakavarga; the specific strength-matrix calculated for the planet Mars</note>: the points for Taurus are 3, for Virgo are 5, and for Capricorn are 0. Because of the zero, the reduction is not applied. Thus, for Taurus: 3, Virgo: 5, and Capricorn: 0 are kept exactly as they were. Similarly, in the Eight-fold Matrix of Mercury <note>term: Budhashtakavarga</note>, the points for Taurus are <margin>•</margin> 7, for Virgo: 3, and for Capricorn: 0. Here also, because of the zero in one place, there is an absence of trinal reduction. Therefore, the values for Taurus: 7, Virgo: 3, and Capricorn: 0 are established exactly as they were in those three positions. This should be observed in Mercury's matrix and also in Mars' matrix. This is the second method. "When there is equality in all houses, the wise should reduce everything"—this means that if the points in the trinal houses are equal, then everything in those three positions should be reduced. In other words, zero should be placed in all three positions. 

For example, in the Eight-fold Matrix of Jupiter <note>term: Gururashtakavarga</note>: the points for Libra are 4, for Aquarius: 4, and for Gemini: 4. Because the points are equal here, zero is established in all three: Libra: 0, Aquarius: 0, and Gemini: 0. This is the same for the Matrix of Seven <note>note: original: "saptashtakavarga"; likely referring to the combined matrix of the seven planets</note>; as it was established in the matrix, so is the secret explained. This is the third method. 

Now, he describes the opinion of some others: "Whatever is the minimum among the trines, that amount should be placed in all three." <margin>28</margin> This means one should place whichever point value is the lowest in the trines. For example, in the Eight-fold Matrix of the Sun <note>term: Raverashtakavarga</note>: the points for Aries are 4, for Leo: 4, and for Sagittarius: 3. Since 3 is the minimum, it is placed in all three positions: Aries: 3, Leo: 3, and Sagittarius: 3. Similarly, for Taurus: 4, Virgo: 5, and Capricorn: 1. Since 1 is the minimum, it is placed in all three positions: Taurus: 1, Virgo: 1, and Capricorn: 1. And likewise...

If one of the two signs is occupied by a planet and has fewer points, and the second is empty of planets and has more points, then the smaller value is subtracted from the larger value in the empty sign. This is the intended logic. The remaining points after reduction will be in the sign without the planet.

For example, in a planet’s Eight-fold Matrix: **Gemini** <note>original: "Mithuna"</note> is occupied by a planet, while **Virgo** <note>original: "Kanya"</note> is empty of planets. <margin>Sha. 29 22</margin>
The phrase "at night one should discard everything" <note>original: "rātrau sarvamutsṛjed"; this appears to be a technical mnemonic or a specific rule citation</note> implies that in certain cases, one should establish a zero. To explain this according to the opinions of some masters: in the <term>Sun's Eight-fold Matrix</term> <note>original: "Surya Ashtakavarga"</note>, if **Gemini** <note>original: "Mithuna"</note> is occupied by a planet and has a higher fruit-value of 4, and **Virgo** <note>original: "Kanya"</note> is without any planets, the result of the reduction is that the sign without the planet loses its points. Therefore, for Gemini, the points are maintained at 4, while for Virgo, the value is established as zero. This follows the opinion of certain masters regarding the Sun’s matrix in **Scorpio** <note>original: "Vrishchika"</note>.

Similarly, in the <term>Venus Eight-fold Matrix</term> <note>original: "Shukra Ashtakavarga"</note>, if **Gemini** is occupied by a planet and has a value of 2, while **Virgo** is without a planet and thus loses its value, then for Gemini the value is established as 4 <note>The manuscript appears to show a discrepancy in the numbers here, perhaps a scribal error or a specific calculation step</note>, and for Virgo, the value is zero.

Again, according to some masters: in the Sun's matrix in Scorpio and the Venus matrix, if **Gemini** is with a planet and has a value of 2, and **Virgo** is without a planet and becomes fruitless—then for Gemini the value is established as 2 and for Virgo it is zero.

Regarding the rule "if both are occupied, no reduction should ever be performed": the meaning is that if both signs [ruled by the same planet] contain planets, then no reduction <note>original: "shodhana"</note> is to be done. For example, in the <term>Mercury Eight-fold Matrix</term> <note>original: "Budha Ashtakavarga"</note>, if **Aries** <note>original: "Mesha"</note> is occupied by a planet with 1 point and **Scorpio** is also occupied by a planet with 1 point, there is no reduction. Thus, Aries remains 1 and Scorpio remains 1; both values are established as they are.

Furthermore, if both signs are without planets, the rule "one should discard the whole" applies. This means that if both signs lack planets and have equal values, the whole value is discarded and zero is established for both. For example, in the <term>Moon’s Eight-fold Matrix</term> <note>original: "Chandra Ashtakavarga"</note>, if **Sagittarius** <note>original: "Dhanu"</note> and **Pisces** <note>original: "Meena"</note> are both without planets, or if there is only one point, in this view, zero should be established.

However, if one sign has more points than the other, then after subtracting the smaller value from the larger value, the remainder should be established. For example, in the <term>Jupiter Eight-fold Matrix</term> <note>original: "Guru Ashtakavarga"</note>, if **Sagittarius** and **Pisces** are both without planets, and Sagittarius has 1 point while Pisces has 4 points: after establishing the reduction for both, Sagittarius becomes [zero] and Pisces becomes 3.

Similarly, in the <term>Saturn Eight-fold Matrix</term> <note>original: "Shani Ashtakavarga"</note>, if **Sagittarius** has 2 points and **Pisces** has 5 points, and <margin>22</margin> both are without planets, then the value of Sagittarius (the smaller value) is subtracted from the value of Pisces. Thus, the reduced value for Sagittarius is established as 1 <note>This calculation seems to follow a specific variant of the reduction rules where the remainder is distributed or handled differently than modern standard Jyotisha</note> and for Pisces as 3.

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the Ashtakavarga system, specifically detailing the second stage of reduction (Ekadhipatya Shodhana) for signs ruled by the same planet, followed by the "Multiplication by Multipliers" (Gunaka) process. An example of **Jupiter’s Eight-fold Matrix** <note>original: "gurora-aṣṭakavarga"; Jupiter's specific point-grid</note>: following the same principle, regarding signs with planets and those without, one should reduce them all. The meaning of "from the one with planets" is this: if one sign of a pair <note>referring to pairs like Gemini/Virgo, which are both ruled by Mercury</note> is occupied by a planet and the other is without a planet, and the "fruit-values" <note>original: "phala"; the points assigned to a sign</note> of both are equal, then the value in the sign with the planet should be kept as it is. 

For instance: here, in the sign of **Gemini** <note>original: "Mithuna"</note>, there is a planet present, and the value located there is 3. In **Virgo** <note>original: "Kanya"</note>, which is without a planet, the value is also 3. Because the values are equal, the value for Gemini is established as 3, while the sign without the planet, Virgo, is established as having zero points. 

Regarding the signs **Cancer** <note>original: "Kulira/Karkata"</note> and **Leo** <note>original: "Simha"</note>, which are handled separately <note>these signs are ruled by the Moon and Sun respectively and do not have a dual-lordship partner</note>, one should set down their individual values, and after the reduction is complete, one should "increase" them by the **Sign Multiplier** <note>original: "rāśimāna"</note>. 

The meaning of "increase by the Sign Multiplier" is this: one should multiply the results of the <term>Reduction for Dual Lordship</term> <note>original: "ekādhipatya śodhana"</note> by the specific multiplier assigned to that pair of signs. Furthermore, for a sign occupied by a planet, one should multiply the reduced fruit-value by the **Planet Multiplier** <note>original: "graha-guṇaka"</note>. "Increase" here means "to multiply." 

Below is the example written out for the **Sun’s Eight-fold Matrix** <note>original: "raveḥ svāṣṭavarga"</note>:

| 3 (Gemini) | 4 (Cancer) | 5 (Leo) | 6 (Virgo) | 7 (Libra) | 8 (Scorpio) | 9 (Sagitt.) | 10 (Capri.) | 11 (Aqua.) | 12 (Pisces) | 1 (Aries) | 2 (Taurus) |

| 5 | 8 | . | . | . | . | | | | | 7 | 15 |

The sequence for writing this is as follows: The Sun is in Gemini, so the values for Gemini and its partner sign are written. Then the results of the <term>Reduction for Dual Lordship</term> are noted, followed by the **Sign Multipliers**. Then, these are multiplied by those multipliers to get the "Sign-Reduced Values." Next, the planets situated in those signs are noted, followed by the **Planet Multipliers**. These are then multiplied by the planet multipliers to find the "Planet-Occupied Sign Values." In this sequence, having established the remainder after reduction, one multiplies by the Sign Multiplier. In the Sun's Eight-fold Matrix, for a sign occupied by a father-planet <note>referring to the Sun</note>, the sign-multiplied value is 9.

The meaning of this is: one should add together those points extracted from the signs that have been multiplied by the previously mentioned Sign Multipliers. And thus, one should multiply by seven; then, by dividing by twenty-seven, the result becomes the years and so on. By multiplying the remainder by twelve and so on, one obtains the months, half-days, and minutes <note>original: "ghatika"; a unit of 24 minutes</note>.

3. Multiply the remainder (15) by 12: 180.

The meaning is this: The measure of a cycle <note>original: "mandala"</note> is 27 years. Half a cycle is 13 and a half years. If the calculated amount exceeds one cycle, one should subtract it from the "Earth" value of fifty-four.

<margin>Sha 31</margin>

# Calculations for Longevity

**Lifespan of the Ascendant:** 21 years, 9 months, 2 days, 0 minutes. Since this does not exceed the cycle <note>original: "mandala"; a 27-year cycle</note>, the lifespan remains as it is: **21 | 9 | 2 | 0**.

Now, the **lifespan for Venus**: 7 years, 5 months, 33 days, 2 minutes. Because it exceeds the cycle <note>The text suggests a comparison or subtraction here</note>, when subtracted from the "Earth" value <note>original: "bhumi"; here referring to the 27-year cycle constant</note>, the result is **12 | 6 | 5 | 5**.

Now, the **lifespan for Saturn**: 15 years, 10 months, 0 days, 1 minute. When subtracted from the "Earth" value, the result is **11 | 10 | 0 | 1**.

Now, the **lifespan for the Ascendant** <note>re-calculated or adjusted</note>: 12 years, 4 months, 16 days, 4 minutes. When subtracted from the "Earth" value, the result is **4 | 7 | 32 | 2**.

These are the "adorned" <note>refined/finalized</note> lifespans of the planets. 

## Rules for Planetary Reduction

Next, one must perform the "reduction by half" <note>original: "ardhaharana"</note> where applicable. Reductions should be performed when a planet is in its sign of debilitation <note>the point where a planet is weakest</note> or in other specific weak states:

1.  **Enemy Territory:** A reduction is required if a planet is in an <term>enemy sign</term> <note>original: "shatru-kshetra"; a zodiac sign ruled by a planet's natural rival</note>. This is calculated through the "visible quadrant reduction." Furthermore, a reduction is made if a planet is defeated in "planetary war" <note>original: "rana-bhaga"; when two planets are in close proximity, the one with lower celestial latitude is considered defeated</note>, or if it is joined with the Sun, Moon, or the ascending node <note>original: "rahu/pata"</note>.

2.  When multiple reductions are applicable, one should not perform all of them simultaneously so as not to "kill" <note>nullify</note> the lifespan entirely. This is called the rule of "mutual reduction" <note>original: "anyonyardhaharana"</note>. When two or more planets are situated in the same sign, the reduction applies to the average of their collective lifespan.

The meaning is this: if a planet is in its sign of debilitation, its contributed lifespan must be halved. When it is in an <term>enemy sign</term>, its lifespan must be refined. Similarly, if it is defeated in planetary war, or joined with the Sun, Moon, or Rahu, its lifespan must be refined.

### Reduction by Visibility

Regarding the <term>visible quadrant reduction</term> <note>original: "drigh-paga-harana"</note>: this refers to the signs starting from the one currently rising (the Ascendant) up through the 12th, 11th, 10th, 9th, 8th, and 7th houses. These are designated as the "visible" portion of the zodiac <note>original: "drig-paga-samjna"</note>. 

For planets situated in these houses, a loss of lifespan must be calculated according to the established rules. This is known as the <term>half-cycle reduction</term> <note>original: "chakrardha-hani"</note>. As stated previously: 

*   One should carefully remove portions of the lifespan: half, one-third, one-fourth, one-fifth, or one-sixth part, depending on the house.
*   It is settled that "benefic" <note>gentle</note> planets lose only half of what "malefic" <note>cruel</note> planets lose.
*   A **cruel planet** <note>such as Saturn or Mars</note> in the **11th house** loses half of its lifespan, while a **gentle planet** loses only half of that (one-fourth).
*   In the **10th house**, a planet loses one-third.
*   In the **9th house**, it loses one-fourth.
*   In the **8th house**, it loses one-fifth.

<summary>This page describes the mathematical "refinement" of planetary lifespans, specifically how to reduce the years contributed by planets based on their weakness, such as being in an enemy sign or in the visible half of the sky.</summary>
<keywords>Ayurdaya, planetary reduction, enemy sign, debilitation, visible quadrant, house deductions, Indian astrology</keywords>

--- Page 65 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical application of "reductions" (Harana) to the calculated lifespans of the planets. Having established the "cycle-purified" years on the previous page, the author now explains how to penalize these years based on unfavorable planetary positions (such as being in the 7th house or being in an enemy's sign), citing the Brahma-Yamala as an authority.</meta>

A planet located in the **seventh house** <note>the house directly opposite the Ascendant, often associated with relationships or opposition</note> reduces the lifespan by **one-sixth**. In these positions, a <term>benefic planet</term> <note>original: "shubha-graha"; naturally auspicious planets like Jupiter or Venus</note> reduces the value by half. If multiple planets are situated in a single house, the reduction is applied to the one that is the strongest.

According to the **Brahma-Yamala** <note>an ancient and authoritative collection of esoteric knowledge and astrology</note>, when a planet is subject to multiple potential reductions—such as being in a sign of <term>debilitation</term> <note>original: "nicha"; the zodiac sign where a planet is at its weakest</note> while also occupying an unfavorable house—one should not apply all reductions cumulatively. Instead, the rule of "greatest reduction" applies: only the single largest reduction should be carried out.

### Illustrative Examples

**The Sun**
The Sun's cycle-purified longevity <note>original: "mandala shuddha ayu"</note> is calculated as **10 | 5 | 18**. Because the Sun is conjunct <note>positioned with</note> Mercury, a "reduction by half" is required. In this specific calculation, the reduction accounts for the entirety of the value.
**The Sun's final refined lifespan: 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0**

**Mercury**
The cycle-purified longevity for Mercury is **7 | 12 | 18 | 4**.
1. Because Mercury is conjunct the Sun, a "reduction by half" is triggered.
2. Additionally, Mercury is in the **twelfth house** from the Ascendant, which also triggers a "reduction by half" for a benefic planet.
Following the rule of the greatest reduction, only one "half-reduction" is applied rather than both.
**Mercury's final refined lifespan: 3 | 21 | 31 | 0**

**The Moon**

A one-third reduction <note>original: "trityamsha"</note> of **6 | 4 | 33 | 20** is considered. After the subtraction:
**The Moon's final refined lifespan: 18 | 10 | 10**

### The Final Calculation of Planetary Periods

Now, to find the final planetary period, take the complete values and multiply them by the number **324** <note>the text uses the symbolic word "roga" (disease) to represent the number 324</note>. Then, divide the result by **365** <note>the text uses the symbolic word "matanga" (elephant) to represent 365, the number of days in a year</note>. This will yield the <term>unrefined lifespan</term> <note>original: "ashuddha-ayu"</note> without a doubt. 

By converting these into days and months as shown previously, one arrives at the correct values. In this way, one should calculate the <term>planetary periods</term> <note>original: "dasha"; the specific spans of time in a person's life ruled by a particular planet</note> for all the planets. 

To summarize the method: take the totals, multiply by **324** (the "disease" number), and divide by **365** (the "elephant" number). The quotient obtained is the refined lifespan; this constitutes the division of the planetary periods. By converting the whole into days and months, the lifespan results for the various planetary periods are established.

**An Example Calculation:**

2. Divide this by the "elephant" (365).
3. The quotient is **17 years**. 
4. The remainder is 179. Multiply this by 12 (months), giving 2148. 
5. Divide by 365 to get **5 months**. 
6. The remainder is 187. Multiply by 30 (days), giving 5610. 
7. Divide by 365 to get **15 days**. 
The remainder is 215. Multiply by 60... <note>The text cuts off here as it moves to the next page</note>

->**Clarification of the Ascendant Lifespan within the Fractional Lifespan System**<-

One should assign years equal to the number of zodiac signs <note>original: "rashi"</note> of the rising sign, and from the remainder, calculate the months and so on; thus the lifespan for the **Ascendant** is indicated. From the degrees and so on, by applying the <term>rule of three</term> <note>original: "trairashika"; a mathematical proportion used to find an unknown fourth value</note>, take the result in months and so on and add it to the months; there the Ascendant lifespan becomes accurate.

In this example, the previously calculated pure lifespan for the Ascendant is **12 | 6 | 13 | 58**. The true Ascendant is **3 | 8 | 45 | 18**. Here, 3 years (equal to the number of zodiac signs) are added to the year-place, making the years **17**. The remainder is 0 | 8. 

Here is the proportion: If 30 degrees of a sign yield 12 months, then what do the remaining degrees yield? Thus, the remainder etc. is the multiplier and 30 is the divisor. Multiplying the multiplicand 12 by the multiplier in degrees... or, when the multiplicand 12 is multiplied by the degrees etc., the result is the same. In this example, therefore, 12 months is made the multiplier and 30 the divisor. Both the multiplier and divisor are reduced by six <note>a mathematical simplification: 12/30 becomes 2/5</note>; thus, **2** stands in the multiplier's place and **5** in the divisor's place. 

Therefore, for the period-lifespan of the remaining signs... multiply the remaining degrees etc. by two and divide by five to obtain the result in months and so on; that result should be added to the month-place. The remaining degrees etc. are **8 | 4 | 4 | 18**. Multiplied by two, they become **16 | 30**. Divided by five, the obtained months are **3**, with a remainder of 2 | 13.

### The Traditional Ashtakavarga System

Now begins the traditional <term>Ashtakavarga</term> <note>A system of Vedic astrology that calculates the collective influence of planets on each zodiac sign through a point system</note>. For the desired planets in the <term>zodiacal wheel</term> <note>original: "rashimandala"</note>, if the count exceeds <unclear>one or more</unclear> cycles, one should perform the <term>Cycle Reduction</term> <note>original: "chakramandala-shodhana"; subtracting multiples of 12 if a value exceeds the twelve signs of the zodiac</note>. This is understood.

The following method should be applied to all planetary excesses: in the zodiacal wheel starting with <term>Aries</term> <note>original: "Mesha"</note>, the sum of the <term>points</term> <note>original: "rekhas"; literally 'lines' or marks representing planetary strength</note> of the planets and the <term>Ascendant</term> <note>original: "Lagna"</note> must be calculated. If the sum exceeds twelve, place the remainder of the cycle reduction into the signs starting with Aries. 

For example, in the <term>Ashtakavarga of the Sun</term>:
*   **Aries Points:** 4
*   In the **Moon's** Ashtakavarga, Aries points: 5
*   In **Mars'** Ashtakavarga, Aries points: 3
*   In **Mercury's** Ashtakavarga, Aries points: 5
*   In **Jupiter's** Ashtakavarga, Aries points: 5
*   In **Venus'** Ashtakavarga, Aries points: 5
*   In **Saturn's** Ashtakavarga, Aries points: 4
*   In the **Ascendant's** Ashtakavarga: 8

Thus, when the points for all planets are placed in the single sign of Aries, the total points are **39**. This is the <term>reduced cycle point</term> 1 <note>original: "mandala-shuddha rekha"</note>. When placed starting with Aries, 2; and similarly for <term>Taurus</term> <note>original: "Vrisha"</note> and the other signs, the same process is repeated for all planets. From this, the auspicious and inauspicious effects are determined for the signs starting with Aries.

Furthermore, as previously described, one must perform the <term>Triad Reduction</term> <note>original: "Trikona Shodhana"; a reduction based on the four elemental groups of signs</note>, and afterwards, the <term>Reduction for Dual Lordship</term> <note>original: "Ekadhipatya Shodhana"; a reduction applied when one planet rules two signs, neither of which is occupied by a planet</note>. 

Then, increase these values by the previously mentioned <term>multipliers</term> <note>original: "gunakara"</note> separately. Having unified them, multiply everything by **seven** and divide the result by **twenty-seven**. The quotient obtained is the <term>Lifespan Total</term> <note>original: "Ayus Pinda"</unclear>. From the <unclear>twelve-fold</unclear> multipliers, one obtains the months, and the <term>ghatikas</term> <note>a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note> are likewise recorded. 

The meaning of this is: first, having performed the summation of all planets in the Aries zodiacal wheel, and having performed the <term>Triad Reduction</term> as before, and then the <term>Reduction for Dual Lordship</term>, one should multiply them separately by the specific <term>Sign Multipliers</term> and <term>Planetary Multipliers</term>. By unifying these, the "Sign Sum" and the "Planet Sum" are produced. The sum of these two is the **Combined Sum** <note>original: "Yoga Pinda"</note>. Multiply that Combined Sum by seven and divide by twenty-seven. The result gives the years. Multiply the remainder of the years by twelve to find the months, and multiply the remainder of the months by thirty to find the days.

Now, regarding the arrangement of the "Friendship-Octet": 

If a planet is situated in an **Angular House** <term>Kendra</term> <note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses of a birth chart, considered the pillars of power</note> and is joined with strength, the longevity results are derived from that. If it is in an angular house, it is joined with the "Padhi-Yoga." The Friend-Octet system is applied during the life periods <term>Dashas</term>; otherwise, the Collective system is used. If the Moon is in an angular house, the longevity is determined by its strength. Within the Friend-Octet periods, the auspicious and inauspicious effects are determined by the strength of the planets. If a planet is not in an angular house, while other planets are...

<summary>This page provides a specific mathematical example of calculating longevity (Ayus) down to the units of years, months, days, and minutes. It concludes the general instruction on longevity and transitions to rules for planetary periods (Dashas) based on house placement.</summary>

# The Determination of Longevity

In this case, the **Individual Octet-System** <term>Bhinnashtakavarga</term> <note>A method where each planet's influence is calculated separately</note> should be determined. Now, regarding the arrangement of **Collective Longevity** <term>Samudayayur</term>: when the **Moon** <note>original: *Shitakara*, "the cold-rayed one"</note> is accompanied by planets and situated in an **Angular House** <term>Kendra</term> <note>the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses, considered the pillars of a chart</note>, or if the Lord of the Center is powerful, then the expert in **Birth-Horoscopy** <term>Jataka</term> should calculate the lifespan using the **Collective System** <term>Samudaya-marga</term>.

If the Moon is in an Angular House with other planets, one should consider the longevity through the rule of the Collective Octet-System; of this, there is no doubt. In this context, if a planet in the center is strong and other planets are also in the center, they possess strength. If they are not in the center, they are considered to lack strength. In such a case, the longevity of the subject must be calculated via the Collective Octet-System. 

When the Moon is in the center and is very powerful, and the other planets in the center are also powerful, then the result is a combination of **Individual Longevity** <term>Bhinnayu</term> and Collective Longevity. From these two, the longevity of the **Life-Period Divisions** <term>Dasha-vibhaga</term> <note>The sequential timing of planetary influences in a person's life</note> is to be achieved. 

If the Moon is not in the center, and the other planets are also not in the center, then between the Moon and the other planets, whichever is more powerful determines the calculation. If the Moon is stronger, the Collective Longevity is calculated; if the other planets are stronger, the Individual Longevity is calculated. The longevity of the time-period divisions is determined in the manner stated above.

## The Chapter is Concluded. Now, the Chapter on Planetary Periods.

When a planet [is determined to be the ruler], its **life-period** <term>Dasha</term> <note>The sequential timing of planetary influences throughout a person's life</note> then occurs. I shall explain this briefly in accordance with the rules of the scriptures. Among the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term>, the Moon, and the Sun, whichever is endowed with the most strength, its period comes first. Following that, the periods of the planets situated in the **Angular Houses** <term>Kendra</term> <note>the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</note> occur in order of their strength. 

The meaning of this is: between the Ascendant and the Moon, whichever is stronger, its first period is determined. After that, the periods are calculated for planets situated in the Angular houses, then those in the **Succeedent Houses** <term>Panaphara</term> <note>the 2nd, 5th, 8th, and 11th houses</note>, and then those in the **Cadent Houses** <term>Apoklima</term> <note>the 3rd, 6th, 9th, and 12th houses</note> in sequential order. If there are two or more planets in these positions, the one with the most strength comes first, followed by the one with less strength, and then the one with the least strength. 

In this specific example, the **six-fold strength** <term>Shadbala</term> <note>A mathematical calculation of planetary power based on six different criteria</note> is as follows: the Sun’s strength is 35; the Moon’s strength is 53; the Ascendant’s strength is 547. Since the Sun has the highest strength in this calculation <note>original: *yadyomamadhye*, likely referring to the mid-heaven or specific calculation point</note>, the Sun's period is first. After that, for the three planets situated in the Angular houses, their periods follow the Sun. For those in the Succeedent houses, such as **Jupiter and Venus** <term>Guru-Shukra</term>, the periods are written in order of their strength. Likewise, for those in the Cadent houses, namely **Saturn, Mars, and the Moon** <term>Shani-Bhauma-Chandra</term>, they are written in the order of their weakness...

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for determining the sequence and duration of planetary periods (Dashas) and sub-periods (Antardashas). It builds upon the previous page’s introduction of planetary strengths (Shadbala) and house positions (Kendra, Panaphara).</meta>

If the planetary strengths should happen to be equal, then those planets that are grouped together produce their results first. For the sake of clarity, one must first consider the assessment of the **Angular** <term>Kendra</term> <note>the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</note> and **Succeedent** <term>Panaphara</term> <note>the 2nd, 5th, 8th, and 11th houses</note> signs.

This assessment is made from the general birth chart and not from the specific house-division chart <note>The author distinguishes between the Rashi Chakra (sign chart) and the Bhava Chalita (house chart)</note>. When the "rays" <note>a measure of planetary power</note> originating from the Ascendant and other points are weak, they become fruitless. These are the same as the calculations in the **Longevity** <term>Pinda</term> and **Natural** <term>Nisarga</term> systems. Regarding the **Eight-fold Strength** <term>Ashtakavarga</term> and related systems, according to the teachings of **Siddhasena** <note>An ancient authority on Indian astrology and astronomy</note>, the **Six-fold Strength** <term>Shadbala</term> should be accepted.

### The Calculation of Sub-Periods

Regarding the **Sub-periods of the Eighth** <term>Ashtantara dasha</term>: in the system of twenty units, or in the **Trines** <term>Trikona</term> <note>the 5th and 9th houses</note>, the wise say that the results "ripen" <term>Pachaka</term> <note>the planet that brings the results of a period to fruition</note>. Among those, one should take half of the two divisions... [3] The meaning of this is: at the end of the primary period, the sub-period of the planet located in the same sign occurs. In the Trines—the ninth and the fifth houses—the sub-period belongs to those signs. In the **Seventh House** <term>Dyuna</term>, the sub-period belongs to the planet situated there. In the **Eighth House** <term>Randhra</term>, it is as has been stated.

In the case of a single sign, or in the Angular and Succeedent house sequences, the one that is bound by two or three factors and is the strongest is the one that "ripens" the results. To calculate the division of the sub-periods, one should perform the "facing distribution." Multiply the two... in the signs, thus the mathematical clarity of the signs is achieved. One should explain this through the "vessel of knowledge" using the stated technical terms. 

The meaning of this is: for the two signs, the two should be multiplied by each other. Multiply the degrees of one by the multiplier of the other sign; and multiply the degrees of the first by the multiplier of the second. By doing this, the mathematical clarity of the signs is established.

Now he describes the method for bringing out the sub-periods:
"The base units... the three are equalized here..." [5] The meaning of this is: at the beginning of the primary period, one should establish the three... then the "ripeners" <term>Pachaka</term> are placed as the resulting doorways. By equalizing them through the method of multiplication and so on, and by dissolving the complex fractions, one should divide the sum by the total of the sub-periods in the primary period to find the internal duration.

### The Calculation of the Eight-fold Sub-Periods

The sub-periods of the eighth <note>original: "astadasha"</note> are explained here. This means the movement occurs once per year. Therefore, regarding the **Ripeners** <term>Pachaka</term> <note>planets that bring the potential of a period to fruition</note>, in the form of the **Trine** <term>Trikona</term> <note>the 5th and 9th houses</note> and the **Eight-fold chart** <term>Ashtakavarga</term>, there is no planet in the **Fifth house** <term>Suta</term> among the four.

There is also no planet in the **Seventh house** <term>Saptamaga</term>. Therefore, the **Sun** <term>Ravi</term> should be known as the ripener of the half-period. In this context, in the period of wealth, **Saturn** <term>Shanaishchara</term> is located in the seventh. [The value] is multiplied by 1 and 3. 

The Sun's portion is two signs, multiplied by the Sun’s divisor of 2. By doing this, the time is known by the experts of time. Here also, those who are **Astronomers and Astrologers** <term>Shastrajna-ganakah</term> have stated regarding Saturn: when these calculations are combined, a spouse is obtained. <note>This implies the calculation predicts the timing of marriage</note>. 

Furthermore, the **Root Period** <term>Mula-dasha</term> of the year is [represented by] 1, 2, 7, 1, 13. Multiplied by a factor of 2, it becomes 2, 4, 5, 14, 26, 42. Divided by the divisor of 11, the resulting **Sub-period of Wealth** <term>Arthantara-dasha</term> is 17, 36, 15, 42. By the Sun's multiplier of 3, the **Mercury** <term>Budha</term> root period of 32, 7, 3, 10 is divided by 11. The result is 10, 55, 9, 21, which is the **Sun’s sub-period** <term>Raver-antardasha</term>.

Similarly, using the **Jupiter** <term>Guru</term> multiplier of 2, the Mercury period of 32, 7, 1, 13 is multiplied to get 64, 14, 2, 14. Divided by 11, the result is 2, 28, 47, 17, which is the **Jupiter sub-period** <term>Guror-antardasha</term>. In this manner, the sub-periods of the planets are to be calculated. This is the naming of the table of sub-periods.

In this chart, there is no planet in the **Angular houses** <term>Kendra</term> <note>the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</note>. Because of this, the sequence of the eight-fold periods is written here according to the principle of the **Individual Eight-fold Strength** <term>Bhinnashtakavarga</term>.

### The Results of the Eight-fold Strength Chart

Now, the results of the Eight-fold Strength <term>Ashtakavarga Phalani</term> are given. According to the sequence of the Eight-fold Strength verses, beginning with the Sun, the numbers should be calculated in the order of the sub-periods. 

1. Write the **Zodiacal Chart** <term>Rashichakra</term> on the ground.
2. One should determine the results quickly through the difference in death <note>likely referring to the terminal points of a period</note> and the sequence of the units.
3. From the Sun to the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term>, one should point out the strength in the Eight-fold charts of the planets according to their specific divisions. 

Regarding the Sun’s portion: a person born when the Sun is in its **Sign of Debilitation** <term>Neecha-griha</term> <note>Libra for the Sun</note> will be joined with disease. One who is afflicted by illness will undoubtedly find no relief here. 

**The Point System:**
*   **One, two, or three points** <term>Phala</term>: These results are considered bad and lead to decline.
*   **Four points**: These results are **Middling** <term>Madhyama</term> or average.
<note>The text cuts off before describing 5-8 points, which are traditionally auspicious.</note>

There is pain and the agony of a broken thigh <note>original: "urubhangirvyatha"</note> when the points are low. There is a loss of one’s work and possessions within this system at certain counts; at twenty-six points, quarrels arise on all sides. ## Thus ends the consideration of the results of the points.

These results are declared specifically for the **Houses of Increase** <term>Upachaya</term> <note>The 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th houses, which improve over time</note>. If one's own Ashtakavarga is auspicious according to the previously mentioned path, (26) then the results are multiplied by ten; the wise know that otherwise, it will be different.

Now, we consider the **Body and Form** <term>Shariramurtti</term> and the results of the planets in their respective houses. (27) From the first house, one understands the perfection of the body, its constitution, and its yogas. From the second house, they know of truth, fortune, and wealth. (28) One's own strength and valor, as well as matters regarding brothers, are in the third. In the fourth house are matters of mother, wealth, and the happiness of children. (29)

From the fifth house, one understands the increase of auspiciousness and intelligence. One should examine the sixth house for the nature of enemies and the condition of children <note>The text here attributes children to the 6th, though traditionally they belong to the 5th; this may reflect a specific lineage of interpretation</note>. (30) From the seventh house, they know of travel and the fortune of the spouse. For the diseases and death of men, one looks to the eighth house. (31) In the ninth house, which is the house of the **Guru** <note>teacher or mentor</note>, they know of fortune and religion <term>Dharma</term>.

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of astrological houses and planetary influences, specifically transitioning from the general characteristics of the twelve houses to the specific significations (Karakas) of each planet, and finally introducing the construction of the Ashtakavarga (Eight-fold Division) grid used for predicting transits.</meta>

### Results of the Tenth House

In the tenth house, which pertains to one's professional life and actions <term>Karmavritti</term>, power, manliness, and happiness are produced (33). Fame arises there, and laughter resides in the homes of those with such a placement. Prosperity, the acquisition of wealth, and helpfulness are the fruits of this house (34). 

The "field" of the wife <note>the 7th house</note> and the "place of sin" <note>likely referring to the malefic houses</note> should be examined. In the house of expenditure <note>the 12th house</note>, one should contemplate <note>original: "vivintayet," a non-standard spelling of "vichintayet"</note> the results (34). Thus, a wise man should discern the twelve houses. When houses are joined by malefic planets <term>Papa</term>, the potential of those houses moves toward destruction (35).

Benefic planets <term>Saumya</term> are known to be the bringers of success, while mixed planets provide mixed results. In the sixth, eighth, and twelfth houses, the auspicious and inauspicious effects are generally reversed (36). A planet, even if malefic, desires to do good when situated in its own home or a friend’s home. Conversely, even a benefic planet desires to do evil when it is in its deepest debilitation or is "combust" <term>Mudha</term> <note>literally "foolish" or "blinded," referring to a planet being too close to the Sun's light to be seen</note> (37). 

Along with these various results, the effects of the **Planetary Periods** <term>Dashaphala</term> are also declared.

### The Consideration of the Houses

The Sun’s influence produces results concerning one's internal power, strength, and matters related to the father. From the Moon <note>original: "mriganka," meaning "deer-marked," a poetic name for the moon</note>, one considers honor, the state of the mind, and matters regarding the mother (38).

Through Mars, one should contemplate the qualities of brothers and matters concerning the land. Through Mercury, one should contemplate wisdom, speech, and the technical understanding of wealth (39).

Through Jupiter, one looks for status, vehicles, fame, clothing, and various colors, as well as bodily growth, intelligence, and the prosperity of all one's goals (40). 

In Venus, one should look for marriage rites, places of pleasure, vehicles, and the physical well-being of the women in the household (41). 

When planets are weak at the time of birth, they always remain so; the results for the home and wealth will be lacking or even contrary to expectations (42). One should declare the results of the planets within their respective categories. However, without the **Eight-fold Division** <term>Ashtakavarga</term>, it is not possible to truly know these things (43).

### The Eight-fold Division of Transits

<note>This section begins the technical instructions for drawing the Ashtakavarga chart.</note>

One should draw nine lines facing East and three or four lines crosswise. With four additional corner sections, the "Eight-fold" chart is formed (1). This Eight-fold Division of transits <term>Gochara</term> is calculated for the Sun, Venus, Mars, Mercury, the Moon <note>original: "shitanshu," meaning "cool-rayed"</note>, and the Ascendant <term>Lagna</term> (2). 

Beginning from the zodiac sign where the Sun is positioned, in the eight-fold grid, for the Sun and the other planets, as well as for the Ascendant... (3)

One should declare the results in the western cells <note>original: "koshteshu," referring to the squares in a grid</note> which extend toward the east, and likewise in the set of eight beginning from the first position (4). In the same way, the results for all planets across all months are declared through these verses (12). These specific results should be proclaimed in their respective places. 

### The Result of the Sun’s Eight-fold Division
#### <note>original: "Gochara-Ashtakavarga-phala" and "Aditya-Ashtakavarga"</note>

Thus ends the results of the Transit Ashtakavarga. Now, the Sun’s Ashtakavarga <term>Aditya Ashtakavarga</term> is described for clear understanding. The ninth sign from the position where the Sun is located is known as the house of the father <term>Pitri-graha</term> (1). One should multiply the results of that sign and calculate the total sum <term>Yogapindaka</term>. Divide that total by twenty-seven; the remainder indicates a specific lunar mansion <term>Nakshatra</term> (2). 

At the time [of that transit], the specific nature of the person is indicated. **At that time, there will be distress for the father; of this, there is no doubt** (3). Or, when the transit occurs in the "trine" signs <note>the 1st, 5th, or 9th positions from a sign</note>, similar trouble for the father occurs. One should know of his death by examining the completion of the major planetary periods <term>Dasha</term> (4). 

When Rahu, Saturn, or Mars <note>original: "Bhuminandina," Son of the Earth</note> are in the third position from the Sun, and Jupiter and Venus are in the house of the son, the man born becomes a "killer of the father" <note>meaning he brings ill-fortune or outlives the father in a way that suggests karmic difficulty</note> (5). If the Sun is situated in the ascendant <term>Lagna</term> or the tenth house, and is joined by malefic planets at that time (5), the wise person who knows time should apply the calculation of the planetary periods <term>Dasha</term> to determine the outcome.

By multiplying this result (223) by 3, we get **669**. When this is divided by twenty-seven <note>the number of lunar mansions or Nakshatras</note>, the quotient is 24 and the remainder <note>original: "shesha"</note> is **21**. This corresponds to the lunar mansion **Uttara Ashadha** <note>The "Later Victorious" star</note>. When Saturn transits through this star or its trinal associates <note>stars located at 120-degree intervals</note>—**Uttara Bhadrapada, Krittika, or Uttara Phalguni**—one should predict distress for siblings <note>original: "bhratru-kashtam"</note>.

Alternatively, the Mars total sum of **223** is multiplied by **7**, resulting in **1561**. When divided by twelve <note>the number of zodiac signs</note>, the quotient is 130 and the remainder is **1** <note>The OCR shows 11 in the text, which corresponds to the next calculation</note>. When the remainder is **11**, it indicates the sign of **Aquarius** <note>original: "Kumbha"</note>. When Saturn transits through the trines of Aquarius, or the specific lunar mansions within that sign, distress for siblings is indicated.

# The Eight-fold Grid of Mercury and Jupiter

<note>original: "Budha-ashtakavarga" and "Guru-ashtakavarga"</note>

Then, when the remainder of the Eight-fold Grid reduction <term>Ashtakavarga-shodhana: the process of refining the points in the grid through specific subtraction rules</term> is found in Capricorn, or in Gemini, one undoubtedly obtains destruction and similar misfortunes. **Calculation for Mercury:** In Jupiter's Eight-fold Grid, the value in Gemini... this is the meaning here: In the sign of **Virgo**, the result is 3 points. This is multiplied by the result, 3, giving 417. When divided by twenty-seven <note>This division relates to the 27 Nakshatras or lunar mansions</note>, the quotient is 15 and the **remainder** <term>Vyarthashesha: the leftover value after division, used to identify a specific zodiac sign or constellation</term> is 12. When Saturn transits the constellation corresponding to this remainder, there is an affliction or "piercing" <term>Vedha: a technical term for a planetary obstruction or negative influence</term> from friends.

Alternatively, the **Total Sum** of 139 is multiplied by the measurement of the affliction, 5, resulting in 695. When Saturn transits **Pisces, Cancer, or Scorpio**, there is great difficulty regarding Mercury’s significations. Thus ends the **Eight-fold Grid of Mercury**.

### Predictions Regarding Children

One should judge matters such as children, wealth, and so on through the method described for Jupiter <term>Jiva: another name for the planet Jupiter, meaning "living being"</term>. Through Jupiter’s Eight-fold Grid, one should specifically determine the number of sons. 1. According to the number of points found in the sign where the planet is situated, so many children will be born. 2. Or, one might consider the lord of the solar or lunar Navamsha <term>Navamsha: a 1/9th division of a zodiac sign, used for fine-tuning predictions</term> to determine the number of children. 3. 

In Jupiter's Eight-fold Grid, take the results remaining after reduction; subtract the points found in signs occupied by "cruel" planets <term>Krura-graha: malefic planets like Mars or Saturn</term>, and the remaining points represent the number of one’s own children. 4. If Jupiter is in the fifth house, the father may have few children. 5. In this way, there are many methods, but time and circumstances must be considered. When many indicators combine, then one should make a definitive prediction.

# The Eight-fold Grid of Jupiter and Predictions for Marriage

According to the characteristics previously described, one should determine the master of the house here. **8** For example: suppose **Jupiter** <note>original: "Guru"</note> is situated in the sign of **Libra**. The fifth sign from it is **Aquarius**, where the point value is **4**. 

Jupiter’s **Total Sum** <term>Yogapinda: a specialized sum of all the points in a planet's grid after two stages of reduction</term> is 84. When multiplied by the point value (4), it equals 336. When divided by twenty-seven <note>Representing the 27 Nakshatras or lunar mansions</note>, the quotient is 12 and the **remainder is 9**. When **Saturn** <note>original: "Shani"</note> transits the constellations of **Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, or Krittika** <note>These three stars are linked together in astrological triplicities</note>, there will be **difficulty regarding offspring** <note>original: "santāna-kaṣṭam"</note>.

When Saturn transits these signs, there is difficulty regarding offspring. This concludes the **Eight-fold Grid of Jupiter**. 

Now, regarding the celestial movements arising from Jupiter’s Eight-fold Grid, and the results for those who are situated therein: **1**

If **Venus** <note>original: "Shukra"</note> is in the seventh house <note>The house of marriage and partners</note>, a man may find a wife from a distant land. If the lord of the seventh house is in a sign ruled by Venus, he obtains a wife from the direction associated with that sign. **2** The birth sign of the wife is known by the sign of the Moon at the time of birth, or the sign of the lord of the seventh house, or the sign of Venus itself. **3** Or, the wife's birth sign may be in a **triangular position** <term>Trikona: the 1st, 5th, or 9th positions in the zodiac, considered to be in harmony with one another</term> to those signs. The wise man should calculate their union accordingly. **4** 

Whether the planet is in its own sign, its exaltation, or in the house of a great friend, the characteristics of Venus should be considered. The lords of the triangular signs from the seventh house and Venus indicate the nature of the woman. **5** O King, when the sign of Venus is favorably placed, there will be offspring. **6** If Venus is the lord of the ninth house and is endowed with strength, the qualities of the wife and children will be excellent, matching the strength of the planets. **8**

When Venus is in a sign of **Saturn** <note>original: "Manda-rashe"</note>, or if Saturn is in the seventh house from Venus, or if Venus is in a debilitated portion, the man desires the enjoyment of a low-born woman. **9** If the **son of the Earth** <note>original: "Medini-tanaya"; a title for the planet Mars</note> is associated with Venus and situated in a house of Mars, the man will be devoted to the wives of others. **10** When Venus is in a division <term>Amsha: a sub-division of a zodiac sign used for fine-tuned predictions</term> of **Mars** <note>original: "Bhauma"</note>, or Mars is in a sign of Venus, and they are joined or aspected by Mars, he will desire the enjoyment of another man's wife. **11** In the house of the wife, if the sign belongs to Saturn and Mars is in the division of...

One should determine the results based on this total sum. **2** Combining the results from Aries to the Ascendant, one should determine the state of the mind and heart. **3** When certain combinations match, it is called a **Death Combination** <note>original: "mrityu-yogam"</note>. After performing the various reductions <note>the "shoddhya" process of subtracting points</note>, one should carefully establish the Total Sum. **4** By multiplying the points of the eighth house and dividing by twenty-seven, the wise person should discard the quotient and look at the remainder. **5** Using that Sum, one should apply the reduction for trines and shared ownership. **6** Establishing that life-force <note>Prana</note>, and dividing the points of the eighth house by twenty-seven, the wise man shall predict the **time of death**.

The **Total Sum of Points from the Ascendant** <term>Lagnam-phalaikyam: the cumulative points or 'bindus' calculated from the rising sign</term> is 17. Through a measure equal to this, one attains wealth, children, happiness, and other benefits.

*   From the Ascendant to the Sun [the result] is 4.

These relate to inauspicious results in the Ascendant’s grid. 

From the Ascendant, the total sum is 12. From **Jupiter** <note>original: "Guru"</note> to the Ascendant, the result is 39; this indicates the **attainment of auspiciousness** <note>original: "Shubha-prapti"</note>. In this manner, one should interpret various outcomes, whether favorable or unfavorable. 

In the **Eight-fold Grid of the Sun** <note>original: "Suryashtakavarga"</note>, the sum from the Sun to the Ascendant is 5; from the Ascendant to the Sun is 43. Through measures equal to these, one experiences **disease and physical ailments** <note>original: "rogadi-prapti"</note>.

Within the Eight-fold Grid, whatever result is produced by the Sun <note>original: "Martanda"</note> when it transits certain points, even the body may suffer pain. This is for one's own sake. Where there are zero points <note>original: "shunya"</note> in the **Eight-fold Grid of Saturn** <note>original: "Shani"</note>, there is no merit. Thus ends the Eight-fold Grid of Saturn. 

From the positions in the Ascendant, the results of the **planetary periods** <term>Dasha: time cycles in which specific planets rule over a person's life</term> are produced for the native.

...from that, **Taurus** <note>original: "Vrishabha"</note> [has] 31, 34, 17, and 34 [points]. **Cancer** <note>original: "Karka"</note> is auspicious. **Virgo, Aquarius, and Taurus** <note>original: "Kanya, Kumbha, Vrishabha"</note> are very auspicious. **Leo, Pisces, and Aries** <note>original: "Simha, Meena, Mesha"</note> with totals of 10, 20, and 18 are the most excellent. 

In the **Tenth House** <term>Dashamabhava: the house of career, social status, and public life</term>, if the number of **points** <term>Rekha: literally "lines," referring to the beneficial points or bindus in the grid</term> is high, there is a great increase in **gains and honor** <note>original: "labha-manam"</note>. If the points are high in this house, one spreads their sovereign power. 

According to the teacher **Siddhasena** <note>Siddhasena is an ancient authority often cited in Jain and astrological literature</note>: "If the points are moderate (5), one should abandon the pursuit, but if the gains are high, one should persist." He whose 10th house has many points becomes a man of enjoyment and wealth. 

**Here is an example:** 
In the 10th house, the points are 18. Because the points in the 11th house <note>the house of income and realized gains</note> are 34, and thus higher than the 10th, there is a slight increase in gain followed by a slight decrease in expenditure. Furthermore, here in the **Ascendant** <note>original: "Lagna"</note>, there are 31 points. Since it has been said that "those with more than thirty points are like gods and bestow auspicious results," the results occur as stated; otherwise, the opposite results should be predicted.

Regarding the **Directional Strengths** <note>original: "Pradakshina"</note>:
The total fruit is divided into three parts according to the sequence. Based on that order, one should know whether the loss or gain is auspicious. Benefic planets in their own houses or in favorable divisions of the three parts indicate that one is a master of the **sciences** <term>Shastra: traditional systems of knowledge or scripture</term> and successful in their work. If the points are low in those parts, it indicates the destruction of wealth.

**In this example:**
For the twelve signs starting from the Ascendant, they are arranged in a clockwise manner. Following the sequence of the three-part cycles mentioned previously:
*   The Ascendant and the eleventh and twelfth signs represent the **East** <note>original: "Purva-dik"</note>.
*   The tenth, ninth, and eighth signs represent the **South** <note>original: "Dakshina-dik"</note>.
*   The seventh, sixth, and fifth signs represent the **West** <note>original: "Pashchima-dik"</note>.
*   The fourth, third, and second signs represent the **North** <note>original: "Uttara-dik"</note>.

By calculating the sum of the points for each of these three-sign groups as previously described, the direction that has the highest total should be declared as the most auspicious. The direction with the lowest total is declared as deficient. 

The measurement of results is imagined in three categories:
1.  **Weak Results:** Totals between 7, 25, 30 up to 54 points are considered to have meager results.
2.  **Middle Results:** Totals between 75 and 90 points have moderate results.
3.  **Excellent Results:** Totals above 90 points are the most excellent.

This is how one evaluates the three-sign groups starting from the Ascendant...

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of astrological mathematics (Jyotisha), specifically focusing on the calculation of directional strength (Dig-bala) and predicting specific life events—such as the acquisition of wealth or the loss of relatives—based on the transit of planets through calculated points in the zodiac.</meta>

The sum of the **results** <term>Phala: the points or "fruits" assigned to houses in the Ashtakavarga system</term> is 26, 60, and 35, totaling 55. Because this exceeds the required threshold, it is considered auspicious in the **East** <note>original: "Purva-dishi"</note>. 

Similarly, for the three signs starting from the **Tenth House** <note>referring to the 10th, 11th, and 12th houses</note>, the values are 39, 30, and 15, with a sum of 105. Because this exceeds ninety, it is considered auspicious in the **North** <note>original: "Uttara-dishi"</note>. 

For the results of the three signs starting from the **Seventh House** <note>the 7th, 8th, and 9th houses</note>, the values are 29, 25, and 30, with a sum of 71. Because this is close to seventy, it is considered **mediocre** <note>original: "madhyamam"</note> in the **West** <note>original: "Pashchima-dishi"</note>. 

For the three signs starting from the **Fourth House** <note>the 4th, 5th, and 6th houses</note>, the values are 30, 15, and 30, with a sum of 71. Because this exceeds ninety <note>The text notes 'exceeding ninety' here despite the sum being 71, perhaps referring to a weighted calculation</note>, it is auspicious in the **North**.

**Benefics** <term>Saumya: naturally "gentle" or auspicious planets like Jupiter, Venus, and Mercury</term> are considered favorable when they are in their **exaltation** <term>Svochcha: the specific zodiac sign where a planet reaches its maximum strength</term> or positioned in the house of the Moon. The success of any undertaking in a particular direction is determined by the strength of the planets situated in that directional quarter. 

**Here is an example:**
In the **East**, if **Mercury** <note>original: "Budha"</note> is in its own house, or if **Saturn** <note>original: "Raviputra," literally "Son of the Sun"</note> is present, or if the **Moon** <note>original: "Chandra"</note> is in its **root trine** <term>Mula-trikona: a high-strength position for a planet, slightly less powerful than exaltation</term>, there will be success in tasks undertaken in the East. However, if they are poorly placed, there is a "mediocre loss." 

If **Venus** <note>original: "Shukra"</note> is in its own **division** <term>Amsha: a sub-divisional chart used to fine-tune predictions</term> in the tenth sign, there is success in work. If **Jupiter** <note>original: "Guru"</note> is in the North, there is "mediocre success." When a planet is in a specific direction, wealth is obtained from that direction. 

Wherever the **Lord of Wealth** <term>Dhanapati: usually the ruler of the 2nd house of income</term> has gone, wealth will be obtained from that direction. 
**For example:** If the Lord of Wealth, Mercury, is in the East, one obtains wealth from the East. If the **Lord of Intellect** <term>Matipati: often the ruler of the 5th house</term> is Saturn and he has gone to the West, then wealth or gains <note>original: "kroshtu," likely a corruption of a word for gains or a specific measure</note> are obtained from the West. 

One must examine the **house** <term>Bhava: the astrological house representing a specific area of life</term> to determine if it will give good or bad results. By the results of the signs and the specific **total points** <term>Pinda: the sum of beneficial points in a house</term>, one determines the outcome. If the "Sauritara" <note>likely referring to a specific planetary transit or calculation involving Saturn</note> occurs, there is a destruction of the house's results.

**The meaning of this is:**
After observing the good or bad results, one should look at the remaining results of that sign. 
**Here is an example:** 
The stated **total points** <note>original: "pinda"</note> for the **Ascendant** <note>original: "Lagna"</note> is 152. This is multiplied by the Ascendant's result (4), which equals 608. This is divided by twenty-seven <note>the 27 Nakshatras or lunar mansions</note>, which leaves a remainder of 23. Counting from the star **Swati** <note>a specific lunar mansion in the sign of Libra</note>, if Saturn is transiting that point, there will be "suffering of the body." 

Similarly, if a calculation for the **Third House** <note>the house of siblings and courage</note> results in the number 4, and if Saturn transits that calculated point, it signifies the **loss of a brother** <note>original: "bhratru-nasha"</note>...

Where the **result for the Ascendant** <term>Lagna-phala: the numerical points assigned to the rising sign in the Ashtakavarga system</term> is equal to 5, and the person is born in the [favorable] lunar day and is free from the influence of malefic planets, the calculation is made for income and expenditure <insert>even for the divine income and expenditure</insert>. <meta>This page continues the technical exposition of astrological mathematics (Jyotisha) from the previous page's discussion on Saturn's transits and their effects on various houses. It moves into the "Bahya-vidhi" (external rules) for the 7th house, specifically concerning the timing of birth or progeny using the Sun's position and the division of the zodiac into life stages.</meta>

->## The External Rule Everywhere<-

The meaning of this is as follows: Take the result obtained from the **trine reduction** <term>Trikona-shodhana: a specific reduction method in the Ashtakavarga system where points are balanced between signs of the same element</term> for the sign occupied by the **Lord of the 7th House** <note>The house governing marriage, partnerships, and progeny</note>. Multiply that value by the points located in the 7th house itself. Divide this total by twelve. 

The remainder indicates a specific sign; when the **Sun** <term>Surya or Ravi: the Sun, used here to time the month of an event</term> transits through that sign or its trines, one should predict the month of hardship or, alternatively, the month of **birth** <note>prasava</note>. This method may also be applied using the planets representing the father and mother. 

### An Illustrative Example

To demonstrate: In this example, the Lord of the 7th house is **Saturn** <term>Shani: also called Suryaja or 'son of the Sun'</term>. He is located in Scorpio, where the result of the trine reduction is 5. Multiply this by the 7th house points, which are 4, totaling 20. When divided by 12, the remainder is 8. Therefore, when the Sun transits through the 8th sign (**Scorpio**) or its trines (**Pisces** and **Cancer**), the birth or event will occur.

### The Three Ages of the Zodiac

The signs are further categorized by the "age" or stage of life they represent to determine the quality of the result:

| Life Stage | Zodiac Signs |
| :--- | :--- |
| **First Age (Youth)** | **Pisces, Aries, Taurus, and Gemini** |
| **Middle Age (Maturity)** | **Cancer, Leo, Virgo, and Libra** |
| **Old Age (Seniority)** | **Scorpio, Sagittarius, Capricorn, and Aquarius** |

If the calculation results in signs associated with "good results," it signifies a special period of happiness and wealth. When the governing planet is also endowed with strength, the auspiciousness is specifically heightened. This is the traditional understanding.
