The total sum within the interval from **Pisces** to **Gemini** is 5. Because this value exceeds one hundred and twenty <note>The text implies a weighted calculation where 5 represents a multiplier or a specific index within the Ashtakavarga system</note>, it serves as a special indicator for happiness in the **First Stage of Life** <term>Prathama-vayas: the period of childhood and early youth</term>. This has been stated as such.

By assigning 3 to the **Moon** and 7 to **Mercury**, and performing the specific reduction, the sum of results up to the sign of **Libra** is 31. This is also a special indicator for happiness in **Youthful Age** <term>Taruna-vayas: the middle stage of life</term>. 

The total sum from the signs beginning with **Aries** <note>Ashvika-adi: starting from the first Nakshatra, Ashwini</note> is 55. This is not more than one hundred; therefore, by subtracting 20, we determine the happiness in **Old Age** <term>Amara-vayas: literally 'immortal age,' the final stage of life</term>. 

### Auspicious and Inauspicious Combinations

A middling level of auspiciousness occurs when **Rahu** <term>the North Node of the Moon</term>, **Venus**, and **Jupiter** occupy the signs (5, 15). In this context, their trines <term>Trikona: signs of the same element, 120 degrees apart</term> are also significant. There is a combination for **Brief Mortality** <term>Alpa-mrityu: an astrological signature for a life-threatening crisis or a short lifespan</term> due to the influence of the **Lord of the 6th House** <term>Ripu-nayaka: the ruler of the house of enemies, debts, and illness</term>. When these combine, the possibility of a "death-yoga" exists.

There are two interpretations for this: 
1. If at birth **Rahu** is in **Pisces** (the sign of the Ascendant), then when **Saturn** transits that sign or its trines, one should predict the occurrence of misfortune <term>Rishta: a period of ill-fortune or health crisis</term>. 
2. Alternatively, if Rahu is in the Ascendant at birth, the same result applies.

In the example provided here, this specific yoga is not possible. 

Regarding the rule for **Brief Mortality**: find the Lord of the 6th house. Whichever sign or **lunar mansion** <term>Nakshatra</term> is occupied by the Lord of the 6th, or where Rahu, Venus, or Jupiter are located—when Saturn transits that sign, that Nakshatra, or their trines, the possibility of death should be declared. 

In this specific example, the Lord of the 6th house is **Jupiter**, who is located in the sign of **Libra** and the **Svati** Nakshatra. Therefore, when Saturn transits the sign of Libra and the Svati Nakshatra, or their trines, one should predict "Brief Mortality" or the possibility of death. This may also pertain to one’s own residence or to a person of equal status to oneself.

### The 30th Division Calculations

In this system, when Jupiter is in the 30th division <term>Chakrakana or Trishansha: a 1-degree or 1/30th sub-division of a sign</term>, or in its trine, and during the year of the "Lord of the Father" <note>Pitari-ishvara: likely referring to the planetary period of the Sun or the ruler of the 9th house</note>, there will be disputes, travel to foreign lands, and physical suffering equivalent to death (5).

Furthermore, considering the **Moon**, the **Earth** <note>Mars</note>, and the Ascendant: if Jupiter is in the 30th division of the Ascendant at birth, and three planets are in the 30th division of the sign occupied by the Lord [of the house]... when Saturn transits the trine of that position, in that year there will be disputes, foreign travel, and physical suffering.

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of Alpa-mrityu (brief mortality) and the mathematical methods used to calculate the specific timing of death or significant life crises based on the positions of the Sun, Saturn, and Rahu.</meta>

...death is said to be equal to the suffering caused by those who are present. In this context, an example is not impossible. Now, regarding the sign of the Sun at the time of death: it is calculated through the twelfth-division <term>Dvādashāmsha: a 2.5-degree subdivision of a zodiac sign</term>. 

The position of the **Sun** in the trine <term>Trikona: an auspicious 120-degree relationship between signs</term> of the **Lord of the House of Death** <term>Mrityu-nātha: the planet ruling the 8th house of the horoscope</term> is considered. The calculation is as follows: multiply the **Solar minutes** <term>Arka-liptā: the Sun's longitudinal position converted entirely into minutes of arc</term> by the **minutes of Rahu** <note>original: "Rahu-lipta"</note>, and divide the product by the **minutes of the zodiacal circle** (21,600). The result is related to the 27 Nakshatras <term>Lunar mansions</term>.

The measurement of **Mars** in minutes should then be processed; when the result is added to the **Sun's** position, the resulting longitude is obtained. This should be understood to occur in the trine of the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna: the sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</term>. 

The month of the event corresponds to the difference in degrees. Below this, it is said that **Saturn** acts as the Lord of the House of Death. In whichever twelfth-division the Lord of the House of Death is situated, the "Asura" **Rahu** <note>The text refers to Rahu as "Asura," a traditional mythological title for the shadow planet</note> is placed in the corresponding trine. Alternatively, one should observe the Sun’s position in the trine of the Lord of the House of Death. Otherwise, the minutes of Rahu should be used as the multiplier. The solar minutes are multiplied and then divided by the minutes of the zodiacal circle (21,600). The resulting value is added to the solar minutes. After performing the calculation for the specific breath-rhythm <term>Prāṇa: a subtle unit of time or energy</term>, the time of death should be predicted through the trine of that shadow-planet <note>Rahu</note>.

### An Example

In this birth chart, the **Lord of the House of Death** is **Saturn**. He is located in the 5th twelfth-division in the sign of **Pisces**. In the trine of this position (12, 48), the "Asura" **Rahu** is placed. Since the Lord of Death is in a specific sign, the Sun's position in that trine (8, 95) should be considered. 

In another case, the **Sun** is at 2 signs, 4 degrees, and 18 minutes. Its multiplier in minutes is 3,968. When this is multiplied by the Rahu multiplier of 2,659, the product is 3,915,588. Dividing this by the **zodiacal minutes** (21,600), the quotient is 18 minutes and 15 seconds <term>Kala: minutes of arc</term>, with a remainder of 588. Multiplying the remainder by sixty gives 540,480; dividing this by the zodiacal minutes (21,600), the quotient is 25. Thus, the result in minutes and seconds is 18' 15" 25"'. 

When this is added to the solar multiplier of 3,958, the sum is 5,543' 25"'. This corresponds to a position of 3 signs, 1 degree, 23 minutes, and 25 seconds. The Sun's position should be subtracted from this total. Regarding the measurement of Mars: the **Solar minutes** are 28,958 and the **Mars minutes** are 1,850. Their product is 245,703,118. Dividing this by the zodiacal minutes (21,600), the result in minutes and seconds is 13' 11"...

### The Chapter on the Results of the Eight-fold Chart System
<note>original: "Ashtakavarga-phala-adhyayah"</note>

Now follow the verses from the chapter describing the collective results. In the **Collective Eight-fold Chart** <term>Sarvashtakavarga</term>, one calculates the distribution of points to determine what is inauspicious and what is truly auspicious. From the starting point, one should examine the total points to determine the proper timing for **travel** <term>yatra</term> and **marriage** <term>vivaha</term> as previously stated. This "Collective Chart" is said to encompass the results of all actions. Without this knowledge, the results of one's wealth and destiny cannot be known.

*Verse 3:* Those **zodiac signs** <term>rashi</term> that possess more than thirty points are providers of auspiciousness. Signs with between twenty-five and thirty points are remembered as being of middling quality.

*Verse 4:* Those signs that have very few points are sharp with hardship and bring distress. One should perform all auspicious works only when the planets are in the best <note>high-scoring</note> signs.

*Verse 5:* A wise man should utilize the best signs when calculating **auspicious timing** <term>muhurta</term>. Through the power of those points, whatever is undertaken will lead to success.

*Verse 7:* A wise man should avoid the signs with low points for important timings. Observe that when the points are higher than the middle average, wealth is attained; if the opposite occurs, there will be poverty—of this there is no doubt.

*Verse 8:* One should declare the results of the **planetary period** <term>dasha</term> based on how many points are in the **Ascendant** <term>lagna</term>. The results of the houses are seen through the lens of the celestial light.

*Verse 9:* One should understand the professional status and strength... <unclear>tirohlā nimastave</unclear> ...middling results are attained when the points are immeasurable, according to the consideration of the houses.

*Verse 10:* Three types of results are directed by the eyes of the planets. If combined with **malefic planets** <term>papa-graha</term>... <unclear>de keṃsha karane sthate</unclear>.

*Verse 11:* A sign occupied by **benefic planets** <term>saumya</term> is known as auspicious. For mixed planets, one should declare mixed results. Based on these three types of results, the outcome of the planetary periods is proclaimed.

*Verse 12:* By combining the points from the Ascendant and the sign occupied by **Saturn** <term>Manda</term>, and then multiplying by seven... <note>This describes a technical mathematical reduction used to predict difficult years</note> ...the result is purified.

*Verse 13:* When a malefic planet transits a position equal to that [calculated result], it brings wounds, disease, and the danger of death. This same calculation should be projected from the position of Saturn and the Ascendant. 

[Results of] **Mars** <term>Bhauma</term>...

## Now, the Deliberation on the Results of Planetary Periods and the Royal Yogas to be known.
<note>original: "Atha Dashaphala-vicharah jneya Rajayoga"</note>

Regarding the results produced by the burden of past deeds for those born under auspicious combinations <term>yogas</term> as told in the ancient Puranas: when all these have passed, I shall now clearly state the various results that follow.

If the first planetary period <term>dasha</term> belongs to the Lord of the Day <note>the Sun</note>, it brings obstacles to the father and mother of men; likewise, it causes distress to the body and wealth, even if the planet possesses strength. [Verse 1]

If benefics are placed in the eleventh, third, or sixth houses <note>the "Upachaya" or growing houses</note>, or even if there are malefics, the result is equal. When the malefic influence is small, at the end of a difficult period, one truly attains wealth and children. [Verse 3]

If a planet is situated in its **exaltation** <term>uccha</term>, its own house, the sign of a friend, its own divisional chart <term>varga</term>, or is in a friendly state and occupies a **quadrant** <term>kendra</term>, and is not viewed by malefics, it becomes a beautiful benefactor during its period. [Verse 4]

When a planet is in a debilitated or enemy sign, even if it has moved toward its exaltation or a friend's sign, it generally provides mixed results rather than purely auspicious ones...

**Now, the Determination of Ascending and Descending Cycles** 
<note>This section begins the technical rules for "Aroha" (ascending toward exaltation) and "Avaroha" (descending toward debilitation).</note>
When a planet moves away from its point of exaltation, it is known as "descending" <term>avarohini</term>. When it moves from its point of debilitation, it is "ascending" <term>arohini</term>...

<vocab>पाके (pake: in the ripening/period), दशा (dasha: time cycle), ग्रह (graha: planet), वक्र (vakra: retrograde), आरोहावरोहक (arohavarohaka: ascending and descending), फललग्न (phalalagna: result of the rising sign), निर्णय (nirnaya: determination)</vocab>

<summary>This page details the specific outcomes of planetary periods (Dashas), particularly focusing on the effects of retrograde motion, weakened planets, and the influence of the Ashtakavarga system on a person's fame, wealth, and health. It concludes by introducing the concept of ascending and descending planetary cycles based on their distance from points of exaltation and debilitation.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of planetary results, focusing on the specific categorization of planetary periods (Dashas) as either "Ascending" (Aroha) or "Descending" (Avaroha). It transitions from the general rules found in the previous section to the specific teachings of the *Somahora Prakasha*.</meta>

One should listen to the specific results of the planetary periods <term>dashas</term>, regarding both inauspicious and auspicious outcomes, especially through their aspects. 10. This is the general rule regarding the ascending <term>aroha</term> and descending <term>avaroha</term> states. Now the Knower of Time <note>the astrologer</note> speaks of the specific details.

When planets are in signs that represent "watery" divisions <note>referring to specific zodiac signs like Cancer, Scorpio, or Pisces</note> and move toward their peak, it is called ascending <term>arohini</term> by the wise; when they move away from this state, it is called descending <term>avarohini</term>. 11.

This is determined by the lunar day <term>tithi</term> of the Moon <note>original: "himoshu," literally 'the cool-rayed one'</note>. The path of the moon is called ascending when it moves toward the light and descending <term>avarohini</term> when it moves toward the dark. 12.

Now, concerning the "star-planets" <note>the *tara-grahas*: Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn</note>: those situated in the first six signs from their point of strength are called descending <term>avaroha</term>, and those in the subsequent six signs are called ascending <term>aroha</term> by the ancient masters. 13.

Ascending <term>arohini</term> is the direct motion <term>anuloma</term>, and the retrograde motion <term>viloma</term> is considered descending. For the star-planets, these three types are clearly defined. 14.

These names, "ascending" and "descending," are established by the divisions of the signs. According to the ancient paths, they provide their results in the first or latter parts of the period. 15. Through this ascending state, the results of the period <term>dasha</term> are particularly successful in achieving one's goals, while the descending state requires more wisdom in managing affairs. 16.

### Thus ends the deliberation on the auspicious and inauspicious in the *Somahora Prakasha* <note>Light on the Lunar Astrology</note>. 

### Now for the results of the planetary periods. 

#### First, the results of the ascending <term>aroha</term> period: 

At the beginning of this planetary period, men may reside in foreign lands. Through the favor of kings or through the company of the most excellent priests <term>brahmins</term>, one gains great respect and the acquisition of wealth. 1.

There may be an interest in secret mantras or rituals, or perhaps specific royal decrees. One gains a taste for learning and philosophy. However, there may be constant anxiety regarding conflicts or battles. 2.

There is the possibility of debts, eye diseases, or dental pain <note>original: "danta-pida"</note>. One might suffer from anxiety due to separation from a wife, sons, or friends. Conflict may arise with kings, thieves, or through the power of fire. 3.

**Now the specific results are told:**

Even if one is serving a king, during this ripening <term>paka</term> of the planet, one gains happiness from wife and sons and acquires wealth from the sovereign. One attains various positions and the desire for more wealth. 3.

During the descending <term>avaroha</term> period of the same planet: there is the risk of headaches <note>original: "shiro-ti-roga"</note> and significant loss among one's own kinsmen.

The Sun's period is harmful to men when it is in the eighth division of its debilitated sign <note>the eighth house or a specific sub-degree of Libra</note>; it brings great misery and suffering from wounds... 18.

### The Results of the Planetary Periods

->**The Fruits of the Vedic Periods**<-

Even though there are various groupings, these are the results of the periods <term>dasha</term> <note>A dasha is a major planetary period in Hindu astrology that governs a specific block of years in a person's life.</note>. When the Sun <note>original: "Aditya"</note> and Jupiter <note>original: "dvija-guru"</note> are well-positioned, they grant auspicious results, even if bad effects were expected; they act like the Sun and the Moon acting in harmony. 1.

During the time of the conjunction of the Sun and the Moon, a person becomes a chief minister, highly honored, and a lord of the earth <note>original: "bhudeva"</note>. They enjoy earthly pleasures and are endowed with wealth and a mastery of the various arts <term>kala</term>. 2.

The period <term>dasha</term> of Mars <note>original: "Ara"</note> is said to bring about the accumulation of all wealth. It fulfills all the desires of men and brings favor from kings and significant wealth. 3.

During this time, one attains great honor, the position of a minister, and widespread fame. There is happiness through children and a natural inclination toward the worship of the gods <note>original: "aditi-tanaya," literally 'the sons of Aditi'</note>. 4.

Through the offering of sesame seeds and flowers, one attains religious merit <term>dharma</term> and becomes a leader among virtuous people, well-versed in the scriptures <term>shastra</term>. 5.

There is happiness concerning daughters and the preservation of the family lineage, though the person may also experience a tendency toward sleepiness, laziness, and a phlegmatic <term>kapha</term> <note>In Ayurvedic medicine, the kapha humor relates to water and earth, associated with stability but also lethargy when out of balance</note> temperament. 6.

These are the general results. However, during the specific "ripening" <term>paka</term> <note>the specific sub-period when a planet's influence is most concentrated</note>, there may be anger from women or sons, conflict, and headaches. 8.

When the Sun is in a high or warm position, one gains honor through pride in their lineage. They acquire horses <note>original: "turanga"</note> and find success over enemies and kings. 9.

During the ripening <term>paka</term> of the Moon <note>original: "Vidho"</note>, there may be conflict with one's own kinsmen and suffering caused by phlegm <term>kapha</term> or obstacles in one’s trade and efforts. 10.

When a planet enters the middle of Taurus <note>original: "Vrishasya"</note>, it brings wealth and vehicles to men. They become dear to the gods and the learned <term>dvija</term> <note>literally "twice-born," referring to the priestly or scholarly class</note> and find happiness with their beloved. 11.

### The Results of the Period of Mars

Regarding the movement of Mars:
After observing the six-fold strength and the stars of the planets, whether in their lowest or highest positions, the results should be declared according to their specific divisions. 2. The lords of the periods, when viewed in their ripening time <term>paka-kala</term>, give mixed results to men according to their own strength. 3. At the entrance of the ripening period, if the planet is well-placed, there are various gains; one may find wealth through the king <note>original: "bhupala"</note>, through the study of scriptures, or through fire-related professions. 4. However, there may be fear from kings, thieves, fire, and enemies, as well as distress from paternal uncles and servants. During the period of a planet acting in a low manner <note>original: "nica-pravritte," referring to a planet in its sign of debilitation</note>, men experience conflict with their wives and sons. This is the general result.

### Special Results

During the ripening time of the Red-Bodied One <note>original: "lohitanga," a common name for Mars</note>, if situated in its own trine <note>original: "trikona," a highly auspicious placement in astrology</note>, it is especially favorable. Through one's own wisdom, one attains happiness, lordship over villages, and success through service.

### The Period of Mars in the Signs of the Zodiac

In the ripening <term>paka</term> <note>original: "pake"; the time when the karmic fruit of a planet is experienced during its major period</note> of the Son of the Earth <note>original: "avani nandanasya"; a name for Mars, as he is traditionally considered the son of the Earth in Vedic mythology</note> when he is situated in **Aries**, there will be great enthusiasm, a lack of violence, fame, pleasure, and courage; however, there may also be obstructions to wealth and the arising of enemies. 9.

In the ripening of the Earth-born <note>original: "avanijasya"; another epithet for Mars</note> situated in **Taurus**, one becomes a resident of a foreign land and meets with wicked or harsh circumstances. There will be disorders of "bile" and "wind" <note>referring to the Pitta and Vata humors in traditional medicine</note>, conflict with one's own kinsmen, and the person may become hard of hearing or slow. 3.

In the ripening of **Mars** when it has entered **Cancer** <note>Mars is considered "debilitated" or at its weakest in the sign of Cancer</note>, there is a loss of wealth and victory, and a decline in the circle of one's influence. One suffers distress from fire and experiences grief regarding the wife, children, and the group of relatives; the person may become physically weak or distressed. 8.

When Mars has fallen from its proper place, there is a loss of wealth, a sense of lowliness, and disorders of the mind. The results are harsh for the body and one remains exceedingly restless during the ripening of Mars when it has reached its point of debilitation <term>nicha</term>.

When **Mars** is in its own lineage <note>original: "vamsas"; likely referring to its own sign or a powerful divisional placement</note>, the person becomes endowed with all good qualities during its ripening. Famous and possessing elephants and horses, they are physically strong and respected by the circle of kings. 10.

In the ripening of Mars when it is situated in **Leo**, there is leadership over many people; however, there may be separation from wife, children, and wealth, as well as fear arising from those of the warrior class <term>kshatriya</term>. 

In the ripening of the Son of the Earth in **Virgo**, the person is skilled in good deeds. They will be devoted to virtuous conduct, humble, and endowed with the happiness of a wife, children, intelligence, and wealth. 12.

In the ripening of **Mars** when it has entered **Libra** <note>original: "juka"; a Sanskritized form of the Greek 'Zygos'</note>, the person is bereft of wife and spiritual guides, and becomes physically weak or diseased. Even if they hold celebrations, they may be associated with wicked company and become separated from their divine or fortunate nature. 13.

In the ripening of **Mars** <note>original: "angaraka"</note> situated in **Scorpio** <note>original: "shali," likely a variant or error for 'ali' meaning Scorpio</note>, the person prospers through agricultural work and the wealth of others. Though the person may be wealthy, they may also be talkative and afflicted by fear of fire, weapons, and enemies. 7.

In the ripening of the Son of the Earth situated in **Sagittarius**, men will have devotion toward the twice-born <note>original: "dvija"; typically referring to Brahmins or the spiritually initiated</note> and the gods. Their desires are fulfilled by the king, yet there may be a sudden break in their enthusiasm due to an obsession with a spouse. 15.

In the ripening of **Mars** in **Capricorn** <note>Mars is "exalted" or at its highest strength in Capricorn</note>, there is the acquisition of a powerful kingdom following the tradition of one's family. Whether a son or a king, one obtains wealth from the earth, and gains of gold and gems. 18.

When the king of planets <note>referring to Mars here as the lord of the period</note> has reached **Aquarius** or **Pisces**, through effort there is the accomplishment of work and the acquisition of wealth. Though there may be a slight fear of the king or from the best of scriptures, there is great affection from the company of the virtuous. 13.

In the ripening <term>paka</term> <note>original: "pake"; the time when the karmic fruit of a planet is experienced during its major period</note> of **Mars** <note>original: "kujasya"</note> when it has fallen from its sign, there is the following of wrong paths, a lack of proper conduct, poverty, sorrow, and suffering from disease. One faces fear of lightning and constant worry regarding one's wife and children. 17. 

When the Cruel Lord <note>original: "vakra swami"; refers to Mars in a retrograde or harsh state</note> is well-placed, its ripening brings favors from the king and a constant good state of mind. 18. Conversely, one may suffer from irregular food, physical exhaustion, and residing in a foreign land. 19.

In the ripening of **Mars** <note>original: "bhaumasya"</note> when situated in the best division <term>vargottama</term> <note>original: "vargottama-gasya"; a planet that occupies the same zodiac sign in both the main chart and the ninth harmonic chart, making it exceptionally strong</note>, there is gain through battle and an increase in victories. 20. The person attains great strength, becomes a seeker of wealth, and achieves world-renowned fame. 21.

In the ripening of Mars when it is of no utility or weak, there is certainly a loss of wages, destruction of the intellect, and failure in all undertakings. There is a loss of courage in the midst of battle. 21. 

The period of the **Son of the Earth** <note>original: "bhumeh suta"; a common name for Mars</note> in an inauspicious state brings dejection and various types of conflict. It causes obstacles on account of one's own kinsmen, loss of honor, and the onset of disease. 22. 

When Mars is situated in its exaltation or in a trine <term>trikona</term> <note>original: "trikonat"; the 1st, 5th, or 9th houses, which are considered the most auspicious houses in a birth chart</note>, the ripening of the period for men brings the attainment of a kingdom or high status, the acquisition of wealth and gems, and general happiness. 23.

**Thus ends the results for the period of Mars.**

### The Results for the Period of Mercury

In the period of **Mercury** <note>original: "budha"</note>, a person possesses knowledge and discrimination. They engage in agricultural work and perform good deeds. Their mind is set on the performance of various rituals and sacrifices. They are endowed with wealth and various business pursuits. One becomes skilled as an artisan <term>shilpin</term> and enjoys various festivals. 

One takes pleasure in music, dance, and fine arts. There is attainment of favor from the king. One shows devotion to teachers, performs sacred baths, and speaks with truth. There is the acquisition of mansions and houses. 2. 

In the ripening of Mercury, there is the happiness of gaining a wife and children. However, one may suffer from disorders of "phlegm," "bile," and "wind" <note>original: "kapha pitta samira"; the three biological humors (doshas) in traditional medicine</note>. By considering the relative strength and weakness of the planet, one should predict these good and bad results. 3.

**Thus ends the general results.**

### The Specific Results

In the ripening of the **Son of the Moon** <note>original: "indusutasya"; a name for Mercury, as he is traditionally the son of the Moon and Tara</note> situated in **Aries**, one becomes wise and fortunate, yet may face moments of ill-fortune. One may act as a messenger or servant, yet maintains a good-natured character. 4.

In the ripening of the Son of the Moon situated in **Taurus**, the person is devoted to their mother and possesses a far-reaching reputation. Such a person is endowed with good qualities, though they may face worries regarding their wife and children...

...adorned with gems. 5.

In the ripening <term>paka</term> <note>original: "pake"; the period in which a planet's karmic influence is felt</note> of **Mercury** <note>original: "budhasya"</note>, the Lord of **Gemini** <note>original: "nryugma"; literally 'the human pair'</note>, the person is devoted to many different types of work and is very talkative <note>original: "abhijalpah"</note>. He is surrounded on all sides by his wife, sons, and friends, and he is never lacking in the happiness provided by his mother. 6.

In the ripening of **Mercury** when situated in **Cancer** <note>original: "kulira"; literally 'the crab'</note>, one becomes attached to the arts and earns wealth through them. He lives in foreign lands, is engaged in many difficult tasks, possesses only a small amount of happiness, and faces opposition from his own kinsmen. 7.

In the ripening of the **Son of the Moon** <note>original: "indusutasya"; a name for Mercury</note> when situated in **Leo** <note>original: "simha"</note>, the person suffers the destruction of his father's fame. There is little happiness to be found with his own relatives, friends, children, or wife, and he suffers from a lack of good judgment. 8.

In the interval <note>of this period</note>, through good handwriting, writing, and the poetic arts, he becomes a man of great gravity <note>original: "guru-vaibhava"</note> and as bright as the moon. He subdues enemies through humble conduct and conquers his opponents in battle. 9.

In the ripening of the **Benefic** <note>original: "saumya"; an epithet for Mercury</note> in its **primary triangle** <term>moolatrikona</term> <note>original: "mulatrikonasya"; a specific sign position where a planet is exceptionally strong, for Mercury this is Virgo</note>, the person is fond of traveling to foreign lands. Through his own valor, he becomes a man of great renown, widely famous and capable of striking down his enemies. 10.

In the ripening of <note>Mercury</note> when joined with malefic planets in a fiery trine, there is a loss of happiness regarding cattle and livestock. There are occasions of quarrel, a lack of physical strength or deformity of the limbs, and the person faces the enmity of others. 11.

In the ripening of **Mercury** when it has reached a <unclear>Drikona</unicler> <note>possibly a scribal error for *Trikona* or *Drkana* (decanate)</note>, the person possesses a sharp and superior intellect and is fierce. He is expert in the work of a scribe <note>original: "lipi-karmani"</note>, gains wealth through commerce, but is deprived of his cattle. 12.

In the ripening of the **Son of the Moon** when situated in **Scorpio** <note>original: "kita"; literally 'the insect'</note>, the person is excessively attached to women and easily satisfied by them. He is devoid of good conduct; though he may possess horses, he is abandoned by his own people. 13.

In the ripening of **Mercury** when it has entered **Sagittarius** <note>original: "chapa"; literally 'the bow'</note>, the person becomes a minister and is endowed with virtuous qualities. He gains wealth through agricultural work and cattle and becomes a leader over many people. 14.

In the ripening of **Mercury** when it has gone to its **debilitation** <term>nicha</term> <note>original: "nicha"; the sign where a planet is at its weakest, which for Mercury is Pisces</note>, one experiences the accumulation of debt and a reversal of fortunes. Through association with the low-born, his physical body declines, and he faces enmity and tears. 15.

<note>This verse is fragmented</note> ...thieves... from the scriptures... devoid of luster, dejected and detached. His soul is tormented by the suffering of his relatives, and through traveling to foreign lands, he becomes extremely destitute. 16.

In the ripening of **Mercury** when it has reached its debilitation, <note>even if</note> he goes to foreign lands, he is devoid of good qualities. He becomes cruel, residing in his own home <note>but without peace</note>...

...devoid of gamblers <note>original: "dyūtajanaivihīnaḥ"</note>. 9.

In the ripening <term>paka</term> <note>original: "pāke"; the period in which a planet's results manifest</note> of **Mercury** <note>original: "budhasya"</note> when it has reached its sign of debilitation <note>original: "nīcābhri-gatasya"; Mercury is debilitated in Pisces</note>, the person—having <gloss>abandoned</gloss> marriage <note>original: "vivāha"</note>—is afflicted by the loss of happiness, wealth, and gifts. Such a man is constantly attached to low paths and finds very little satisfaction. 10.

In the ripening of **Mercury** when it has set <note>original: "astopayātasya"; referring to heliacal setting or combustion by the Sun</note>, the man becomes extremely miserable due to his own kinsmen. He may be afflicted by diseases such as cough <note>original: "kāsamayādyaiḥ"</note> and the like; he is filled with worldly desires but remains low and devoid of the wisdom of the Vedas. 11.

When Mercury is situated in <term>Vargottama</term> <note>original: "vargottamatsthe"; a highly auspicious state where a planet occupies the same zodiac sign in both the main birth chart and the ninth-divisional chart</note>, or enters the sign of the Moon <note>original: "himarāṃśi-prave"; Cancer</note>, and is associated with or aspected by both benefics and malifics, the results of the period are mixed. When the **Benefic** <note>Mercury</note> is in a clear or moving <note>original: "cale"</note> state, his period brings knowledge, wealth, and lordship over women and men, creating the highest happiness for people. 12.

This concludes the results of the period of Mercury.

**Now, the results of the period of Jupiter**

Upon the entrance of the period of **Jupiter** <note>original: "guru-daśā-phalāni"</note> in the signs of **Scorpio** or **Leo**, the person becomes devoted to the true meaning of the sacred laws and scriptures <note>original: "saddharma-śāstrārtha-rato"</note>. Their desires are fulfilled through the grace of the self; they possess a natural disposition for good deeds and a taste for Vedic sacrifices <note>original: "yajña"</note>. 1.

He is endowed with the proper rites for the worship of gods and Brahmins, as well as great patience. He is possessed of intelligence and filled with immense wealth, gold, clothes, and vehicles, and is joined with the happiness of a wife and children. 2.

When situated in its sign of exaltation <note>Cancer</note>, even if born in a lower family <note>original: "kauyakule"</note>, one becomes excellent and endowed with the quality of truth <note>original: "satya-guṇānvitaḥ"</note>. However, he may be afflicted by diseases of the abdominal glands, the stomach, or the spleen <note>original: "gulmodara-plīha-ganāmayādyaiḥ"</note>, and may act against his own mind. 3.

Generally, in the ripening period of the **One Worshipped by the Gods** <note>original: "surārcitasya"; a common title for Jupiter</note> when he has reached **Aries** <note>original: "meṣa"</note>, men gain leadership over many. There is a rise in knowledge, wealth from the king, and the acquisition of happiness from wives, sons, and friends. 4.

In the ripening of the period when Jupiter is in **Taurus** <note>original: "vṛṣa"</note>, one may be afflicted by sorrows and diseases. Such a man becomes a resident of foreign lands, destitute, and lacking in celebratory joy, though he remains very courageous. 5.

In the ripening of the period of **Jupiter** <note>original: "jīvasya"</note> when situated in **Gemini** <note>original: "yugma"; literally 'the pair'</note>, there is distress regarding the mother's side or one's own kinsmen. The man becomes sickly, suffers <gloss>separation</gloss> from his wife, and possesses very little wealth while his mind remains anxious. 6.

In the period of **Jupiter** when it has reached **Cancer** <note>original: "karka"</note>, there is the acquisition of a kingdom and great magnificence. Men become experts in the arts and sciences, and find great happiness and favor with the king. 7.

When the **One Worshipped by the Gods** is in his state of highest exaltation, the ripening of his period makes a man...

...therefore, like a young woman <gloss>is startled</gloss> by thunder, he is tormented in that state and overcome by vices. 8.

Regarding the period <term>paka</term> <note>original: "pāke"; the ripening or manifestation of a planet's influence</note> of **Jupiter** <note>original: "guroḥ"</note>, if it occurs in a certain division <term>amsha</term>, men say he will have no authority. The man is burdened for a long time by difficulties regarding the acquisition of a wife and children <note>original: "kalatra-putra-majjajāt"</note>. 9.

If the planet is touched by a malific <term>papa</term> <note>a planet that causes harm, such as Saturn or Mars</note>, the man is led through the gates of kings <note>referring to legal or governmental dealings</note>, but is overcome by financial anxiety. There will surely be occasions for quarrels and constant wandering <note>original: "bhramaṇaṃ"</note> for that person. 10.

Depending on those he relies upon, he may gain through his own virtues, but he remains constantly at odds with his enemies. He may be forced to eat the food of others and becomes a man devoid of discernment <note>original: "viveka-hīno"</note>, perhaps even a destroyer. 11.

He becomes angry, deceitful, and of a very censurable mind during this ripening period. Though capable, he enters into battles and enmities. Even with the enthusiasm of a son, he is overcome by passion and lacks discipline. 12.

If situated in certain angular houses <term>kendras</term>, the man becomes superior in intellect but may be deceptive. He may possess excessive pride and be attached to various vices <note>original: "vyasanānuraktaḥ"</note>. 13.

When **Jupiter** <note>original: "vācaspati"; a name for Jupiter meaning 'Lord of Speech'</note> is in a specific position, men obtain success in all efforts, including agriculture and wealth in cattle <note>original: "kṛṣi-godhanāni"</note>, gaining everything they possess. 14.

If the ripening occurs when the planet is in a state of decay or old age <note>original: "vṛddhasya"</note>, the man becomes one who works for others. He suffers from diseases of the stomach <note>original: "jāṭhara-roga"</note> and abdominal tumors <term>gulma</term>, becoming particularly miserable. 15.

If the strength of **Jupiter** is fallen, there are many faults to be spoken of. Due to the talk of the people, there is wickedness, discord <term>kali</term>, and slander by thieves. 16.

In the period <term>dasha</term> of **Jupiter** situated in the house of **Aquarius** <note>original: "kumbha-gṛha-sthitasya"</note>, one finds prosperity, devotion to learning, and the acquisition of happiness through a wife and wealth, often obtaining these through royal favor. 17.

When situated in **Pisces** <note>original: "mīnāsthitasyā"</note> during its ripening, the man becomes exceptionally wise <note>original: "dhīmān"</note>. Through the pursuit of knowledge, he becomes famous and humble, filled with the weight of love for beautiful women and great joy. 18.

If the period of **Jupiter** <note>original: "jīva-pāke"</note> occurs in the best division <term>vargottama</term> <note>an auspicious state where a planet is in the same sign in different charts</note>, the man is always employed in the world, possesses excessive wealth, but may become arrogant or have a lean body. 19.

### Thus ends the results of the period of Jupiter.

### The Results of the Period of Venus

Now, regarding the period of **Venus** <note>original: "Shukra"</note>: He delights in the company of thousands of young women. There is an acquisition of wealth from various people and the birth of offspring. Men gain the friendship of the God of Love <note>original: "Madana"</note>. One develops a taste for singing, dancing, and music, and becomes an expert in these arts. He possesses a good character and directs his mind toward the scriptures <term>shastras</term> and the giving of charity to virtuous people. He is particularly skilled in the buying and selling of goods. 1.

There is a recovery of wealth earned in the past. He gains gems from women. However, there may be fear or quarrels involving sons and grandsons. He may experience unsteadiness or the shock of a sudden blow. 2.

Infirmity may arise from an excess of phlegm <term>kapha</term> <note>one of the three humors in Ayurvedic medicine</note>, or from external afflictions to the body. He may suffer from anxiety, the loss of a loved one, or enmity with low-born people. These are the general results described for this planet. 3.

### Thus ends the general results.

Now, the results of the period <term>dasha</term> are determined according to the specific zodiac sign in which the planet is born or situated. These results are applied by calculating the relative strength of the planets according to the rules of the astrological scriptures. 1.

#### Venus in the Signs

During the period of the **Son of Bhrigu** <note>original: "Bhṛgu"; a common name for Venus in Sanskrit literature</note> when occupying **Aries** <note>original: "Mesha"</note>:
The person may experience confusion, agitation, or even the effects of poison. He might become pitiless or cruel. While he finds happiness through women and wealth, he remains somewhat fickle. 4.

In the ripening <term>paka</term> of the **Son of Bhrigu** situated in **Taurus** <note>original: "Vrisha"</note>:
He is humble and devoted to the "belly of the scriptures" <note>meaning he is deeply immersed in study</note>. He is engaged in agricultural work, possesses a strong and capable nature, and is full of vitality. He will likely have daughters as offspring and will be a leader among good people. 5.

When the period of the **Son of Bhrigu** occurs while situated in **Gemini** <note>original: "Mithuna"</note>:
He is joined with various poetic arts and festivities. He experiences great wonder and gains wealth through humorous stories and clever conversation. He is eager for the arrival of new positions or status. 6.

In the period of the **Son of Bhrigu** when in **Cancer** <note>original: "Karka"</note>:
He becomes a protector of women, yet he may be somewhat isolated or cut off from others. He will be characterized by the possession of jewelry and wealth, yet he remains perpetually anxious in his heart. 7.

During the period of **Venus** when occupying **Leo** <note>original: "Simha"</note>:
The man is free from fear but may be without a wife. He may possess a dual nature or "two sides." He experiences happiness in his early childhood and may become a physician <term>vaidya</term>. 8.

In the period of **Venus** situated in **Virgo** <note>original: "Kanya"</note>:
There is a celebration of wealth, but the person may lack a certain luster or physical beauty. He may live in a foreign land and be a restless or fickle man, even while accompanied by his wife, sons, and kinsmen. 9.

When the **Descendant of Bhrigu** <note>original: "Bhargava"; another name for Venus</note> is situated in the end of **Virgo**, in the ripening of the period, he falls near the king...

In the period of **Venus** <note>original: "Bhruguja"; a title for Venus as the son of the sage Bhrigu</note> situated in a trine <term>trikona</term> <note>a highly favorable triangular house in a horoscope</note>, a man becomes distinguished and eminent. He engages in agricultural activities and, while possessing some grain and wealth, he becomes a wealthy man who speaks with fierce anger.

In the period of **Venus** rising in Scorpio <note>original: "Keetodaya"; literally 'the rising of the insect'</note>, one becomes a servant to a king, powerful, and devoted to performing work for others. He may be sharp-tongued and a "son of hardship" <note>an idiom suggesting one who endures much struggle</note>, yet he remains a man of wealth and progeny. 12. 

In the period of **Venus** in Sagittarius <note>original: "Dharasana"; referring to the seat of the archer</note>, a man becomes proficient in the arts of poetry and fine arts. He may be burdened by debt and face opposition from enemies, yet he receives protection and favor from the king. 13. 

In the period of **Venus** when it has reached Capricorn <note>original: "Nakra"</note>, one becomes sickly yet remains devoted to his duties. He endures hardships and lives like a virtuous mendicant. He is filled with anxiety regarding his family and possesses knowledge of foreign lands, but his body suffers from an imbalance of the humors of wind and phlegm <term>kapha</term>. 14. 

In the period of **Venus** in Aquarius <note>original: "Kumbha"</note>, the person performs cruel deeds and possesses a bad character. He is a destroyer of his own peace, overwhelmed by vices, and tormented by disease. 15. 

In the period of **Venus** rising in its exaltation <note>original: "Shashakodita"; likely referring to its placement in Pisces</note>, the man becomes a leader and a prominent chief. He is endowed with wealth from agriculture and the pleasures of luxury; he becomes a capable upholder of the law <term>dharma</term>. 16. 

When **Venus** has entered the best divisional position <term>vargottama</term> <note>a position where a planet occupies the same sign in both the main chart and the ninth divisional chart, increasing its strength</note>, the man gains wealth through the power of sacred chants <term>mantras</term> and his own reputation. He exerts effort in heroic deeds, though he may be overcome by an imbalance of the phlegm and wind humors. 17. 

When the **Son of Bhrigu** <note>Venus</note> is situated in his sign of exaltation, or in the tenth house <note>the house of career</note> or eleventh house <note>the house of gains</note>, the man becomes a protector of honor. He rules over territories filled with elephants and gems; he is wise, gifted with vision, and becomes a master of the world. 18.

### Thus ends the results of the period of Venus.

### Now, the Results of Saturn

<note>original: "Atha Shaneh"</note>

The son of the Sun <note>original: "Shani"; Saturn is considered the son of the Sun-god in Hindu mythology</note> makes a man wise and a seeker of both spiritual nectar and wealth. He is granted authority over sons and is devoted to the worship of the gods and the priests <note>original: "dvija"; literally 'twice-born'</note>. He is endowed with gems, horses, and mighty elephants. He is proficient in the art of poetry and the use of weapons. He is distinguished by his great fame and excellent character. This humble man brings happiness to his family and gains joy from land and serpents <note>symbolic of hidden treasures or ancient wisdom</note>.

Suffering from diseases of the wind <note>original: "faniloye"; likely referring to vata or 'wind' imbalances in Ayurvedic medicine</note>, the person will be tormented in their body. ### General Results for the Period of Saturn

When the period <term>paka</term> <note>literally "ripening," referring to the time a planetary influence bears fruit</note> of **Saturn** occurs in **Aries** <note>original: "Mesha"</note>, a person becomes prone to traveling and lives independently. At this time, the body is afflicted by various pains, and the individual may be abandoned by their own mother and kinsmen. 2.

In the period of **Saturn** situated in its sign of debilitation <note>Aries is the sign where Saturn’s power is considered weakest</note>, one becomes effortless or lazy. While perhaps possessing a title, they are prone to quarrels. Such a person becomes destitute and may be deceived by a woman, resulting in a lean or weakened body. 4.

In the period of **Saturn** in **Taurus** <note>original: "Vrisha"</note>, if joined by the **Moon**, the person shines with the honors received from a protector of the earth <term>bhupala</term> <note>a king or high official</note>. They gain special fame on the battlefield and are considered highly intelligent. If **Mars** <note>original: "Mangala"</note> also influences this time, the results are even more significant. 5.

In the period of **Saturn** when it has reached **Gemini** <note>original: "Dvandva"; literally 'the pair' or 'the couple'</note>, the person is devoted to truthful speech and the enjoyment of life's pleasures, remaining very peaceful. However, there is a risk of injury in battle or conflict. Though active in the work of others, the person may become destitute due to the influence of cruel people. 6.

In the period of **Saturn** as it ascends **Cancer** <note>original: "Karka"</note>, a man becomes self-reliant and possesses excellent strength of intellect. However, he remains anxious, wears poor-quality clothing, and suffers from pain in the ears. His body becomes thin. 7.

In the period of **Saturn** situated in **Leo** <note>original: "Panchanana"; literally 'the five-faced one,' a title for the lion</note>, there are many quarrels involving horses and vehicles. Conflict arises with the wife, children, and kinsmen. The person becomes an extreme enemy to their own people and finds no happiness even among the virtuous. 8.

In the period of **Saturn** situated in **Virgo** <note>original: "Pathona"</note>, the mind is focused on the management of one's own side/faction and the home. Through the pursuit of knowledge, one gains fame among their own warriors. Such a person becomes a devotee of the gods and the learned <term>dvija</term> <note>literally 'twice-born,' referring to the priestly or scholarly class</note>. 9.

In the period of **Saturn** situated in **Libra** <note>original: "Tula"</note>, one acquires elephants, horses, and jewels. Gold and fine garments are obtained according to the status of one's family. The person gains fame among the people and victory through the charms of women. In this way, various types of strength and human dignity arise. 10. <note>Saturn is 'exalted' or at its highest strength in Libra, hence the very positive results.</note>

In the period of **Saturn** having gone to **Scorpio** <note>original: "Karthiya" / "Vrischika"</note>, the person becomes merciless and follows a low or mean path. They depend on others for their livelihood and are known for engaging in reckless or daring deeds. 11.

In the period of **Saturn** situated in **Sagittarius** <note>original: "Sharasana"; literally 'the bow'</note>, men are endowed with great wealth and splendor. The person is burdened <note>in a positive sense of responsibility</note> by his children and becomes a recipient of excellent and widespread fame. 12.

In the period of **Saturn** when it has reached **Capricorn** <note>original: "Nakra"</note>, one becomes someone who possesses great wealth and is successful in battle. Surrounded by groups of heroes and family members, the person gains wealth through knowledge, travel, and various traditions. 13.

...having three artistic talents <note>original: "kalashasvitayam"; likely referring to proficiency in traditional arts or skills</note>, such a person is well-behaved and of good complexion. Though they may engage in some questionable deeds, they will eventually become a person of high rank <term>krodhara</term> <note>a title of status or a specific rank within a community</note> and be fully enriched with all kinds of wealth and seals of authority. 14. 

When **Saturn**, the son of the Sun <note>original: "Arkasuta," a common epithet for Saturn</note>, is situated in **Pisces** <note>original: "Mauna," another name for the sign Meena</note> during its ripening period <term>paka</term>, the person becomes the head of a guild or a prominent leader. Based on this strength, the person may travel to foreign lands during that time. 17. 

In this way, the various results for the planets have been proclaimed. Having understood the natural disposition and temperament of the person, the astrologer should then declare the results that correspond to that nature. 18. 

## Thus ends the results for the Period of Saturn

## Now, the results for the Period of the Ascendant

The results—whether they be auspicious, middling, or inauspicious—should be declared according to the lord of the decanate <term>drekkana</term> <note>a 10-degree division of a zodiac sign</note> of the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term> <note>the sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</note>. If the Ascendant is in a fixed sign or its sign of exaltation, the results are considered best; in other cases, they are middling or poor. 1. 

The results for the **Virgo** <note>original: "Kanya"</note> division are described as middling and auspicious. These results are determined by the strength of the lord of that specific division in the sequence of the houses. 2. 

## Thus ends the Eighteenth Chapter of the Light on Auspicious Horoscopy

## Now, the sequence of the Sub-periods

The sequence of the sub-periods <term>antardasha</term> <note>shorter time cycles within a major planetary period</note> is now spoken of in their proper order. 
I have already described the ethics and fame derived from the Ascendant. Now, the reversal of results based on the previous and subsequent positions must be understood. Therefore, one should properly calculate the movement of the cycles. The results are brought forth by the wise through calculations involving the strengths of the planets and their positions in the sixth and eighth houses from each other <note>original: "shashtashtaka"; a difficult astrological relationship indicating friction or challenges</note>. 1. 

Through these various combinations, the sub-periods are determined in a sequence. Some experts in calculation say that the results are fixed by the relative strength of the planets in the divisions of the cycle. 3. 

## Now, the sequence of the Eight-fold Period

## **Now, the Results of the Rays from the Saravali**
<note>The Saravali is a famous classical Sanskrit text on astrology. "Rays" (rashmi) refer to a system of quantifying a planet's strength; more rays generally indicate more auspicious power.</note>

This science of the "Principal Rays" is not known by the followers of the common path; therefore, I shall explain it with effort according to their teachings. **1**

The rays are assigned thus: nine, nine, eight, five, seven, and seven <note>original: "nav-nav-ashta-pancha-sapta-sapta," referring to the specific numerical values of rays assigned to planets like the Sun, Moon, etc., when in their highest positions</note>. These are the five-fold divisions in the auspicious parts. **2**

When planets are mixed <note>distributed across different signs</note>, the science dictates that according to the school of Latacharya <note>An ancient astrological authority often cited in early texts</note>, the seven planets possess a storehouse of rays. **3**

Through the words of the wise, this is known; however, in my own calculation, based on the thunderbolt-like words of the ancients, a planet that has gone into its debilitation <term>nica</term> <note>the zodiacal sign where a planet is weakest</note> is considered to be without rays. **4**

Between the point of debilitation and the point of exaltation <term>ucca</term> <note>the sign where a planet is strongest</note>, the rays should be understood to increase. I shall explain the internal calculation exactly as it is. **5**

Subtract the point of debilitation from the planet's current position, and divide by the total range. If the result is more than six, the rays are calculated accordingly. The total rays are then divided by six. **6**

In the division of a friend's house or an auspicious portion, the rays are as if the planet were in its exaltation. If the planet is in its own house, the value is doubled. This is how the scholars have understood it. **7**

In the division of an enemy's house or when in debilitation, the rays are diminished. If the planet is "combust" <term>astagata</term> <note>original: "astagato," meaning so close to the Sun that its light is obscured</note>, the value is halved, except for the "Vajra" planet <note>likely referring to Venus or a specifically strong planet in this context</note>. **8**

For a planet in retrograde motion <term>vakri</term> <note>appearing to move backward from Earth's perspective</note>, the value is doubled. The calculations should be made by subtracting the portions of the zodiac as previously directed for exaltation. **9**

Those whose rays are unified and multiplied as stated, even if of lowly birth, become ministers or kings. Those whose rays are broken or diminished, even if born in a noble house, become like beggars. **10**

As long as a man's planetary periods <term>dasha</term> possess beautiful and full rays, he remains great. If the rays are of a broken form or have moved to a state of debt, he is born luckless and deprived of fortune. **11**

To the extent that the rays are diminished, the man's greatness is lessened...

<summary>This page concludes a chapter on planetary periods by the author Punjaraja and introduces the "Rashmi-phala" (Results of Rays) from the classical Saravali. It details the mathematical rules for calculating a planet's strength (rays) based on its position, such as being in its strongest sign, weakest sign, or in retrograde.</summary>

...wealthy, endowed with righteousness <term>dharma</term>, and possessed of happiness. He becomes a refuge for all, excelling among his own kin. For those born as merchants, they are virtuous, courageous, and steady in their own professions. With excellent fame, such a man becomes a protector of the earth. **17** This result, which comes from the rays as described, is generally applicable to all people. Those who are most fortunate and wise are distinguished by their character and their own deeds. **17** 

Up to thirty <note>original: "triṃśamita," referring to the total count of planetary rays</note> rays, if they fall as inauspiciously placed in the birth chart, the person becomes a leader of various types of common folk or low-born people. However, for those born among merchant classes whose rays are known to be thirty-one <note>original: "ekādhitriṃśamita," 31 rays</note>, they become prominent in the royal assembly, acting as leaders of various groups. **18** 

In the birth of a man where the source of rays is thirty-two <note>original: "dvija-pramāṇa," where "dvija" or tooth represents 32</note>, he becomes a knower of various ancient scriptures <term>Puranas</term> and the lord of a hundred villages. **19** He becomes like a god, or superior to others through his knowledge of the scriptures <term>shastras</term>; he is devoted to the traditional law-codes <term>smritis</term>. With the acquisition of villages, some born under this count travel to distant lands. **18** 

With thirty-five <note>original: "pañca-pāvaka," where pavaka/fire represents 3 and pancha represents 5, read right-to-left in the Katapayadi/Sankhya system as 35</note> rays, a man becomes a regional ruler <term>mandaleshvara</term>. Endowed with power, vitality, and wealth, he is victorious and possesses a bright and pure fame. **19** 

With thirty-six <note>original: "ṣreṇā-tribhir," 3 times 12, or 36</note> rays, a man certainly becomes a leader of a town or of his own people in sequence, obtaining many villages. He becomes a person of great importance. **1** 

If the number of rays is thirty-nine <note>original: "graha-tri-saṃkhyaiḥ," where graha/planets are 9 and tri is 3, making 39</note>, his power is incomparable and he is highly intelligent. He becomes a lord of travelers and a master of many villages. If the number of rays at birth is thirty-nine, he becomes a king of famous renown, high-spirited and immensely powerful. **21** 

For one whose rays reach the measure of forty-two <note>original: "dvā-dha-pramāṇa," a technical count for 42</note>, he becomes a ruler. Through the strength of his victory, he possesses an army that truly roars across the world, unmatched in physical stature. **22**

...with the rays being twenty-one **23** <note>The OCR shows the number 23, but the text "ekavimsha-tulya" explicitly refers to 21 rays.</note>. If a man is born with twenty-one rays, he becomes wealthy and faces life with a steady disposition, attaining success in all his endeavors.

He becomes a leader of men and a protector **24**. When a man is born with a specific combination of rays <note>original: "yugma-yuge," implying a pairing or specific alignment</note>, it is written in the sacred teachings that he will be endowed with great valor and power. He shall be a leader and a protector of the earth **25**. If the rays are equal to twenty <note>original: "vaishari," likely a corruption of "vimshati" meaning twenty</note> at the time of a man's birth, he becomes a universal emperor <term>sarvabhauma</term> <note>a ruler whose sovereignty extends over the known world</note>. Like the ocean reaching its shores, his influence spreads far and wide **26**. Those rays which are appropriately manifest at the time of birth for high-born men are to be praised. Even others who possess such rays are celebrated for their exceptional fame, shining like lamps across the land **27**.

->**Thus ends the Chapter on Ray Birth in the *Light of Day and Night*.**<-

Now begins the results of planets endowed with benefic strength <term>saumya-bala</term>. In various circumstances, such planets grant skill in managing wealth and protecting the kingdom. When there is an abundance of strength <term>virya</term>, men are characterized by their vigor and they become masters of their households, endowed with potency **1**. Through the high quality of this strength, a person’s status and honor are elevated. During the planetary periods <term>dasha</term> <note>cycles of time ruled by specific planets</note>, such a person experiences an increase in various types of wealth, portions of land, and general happiness **2**.

The strength of the direction <term>dig-bala</term> and the strength of the lord of the year lead to an increase in prosperity, the acquisition of jewels, and the gain of fine cattle. With a broad chest and a noble stature, the person enjoys life, protected by the strength of time <term>kala-virya</term> **3**. Men born when the planets are moving in benefic signs and are endowed with strength are characterized by good conduct, truthfulness, and devotion. They are grateful and possess a handsome appearance **4**.

Conversely, if men are influenced by "cruel" <note>malefic</note> planets that are nonetheless endowed with strength, they may be diligent in their own work but lack shame. They are filled with the quality of darkness <term>tamas</term> <note>the guna or quality associated with ignorance and inertia</note>, yet remain grateful to those who help them. They are deeply connected to their own kin, though they may be prone to conflict **5**. The wise ones should carefully consider these results. In matters of the state, wealth, and power, one must look at the strength of the planet’s motion <term>chesta-bala</term> and the specific combinations <term>yoga</term> it forms **9**.

->**Thus concludes the considerations on planetary strength.**<-

8

Whether the results are auspicious <gloss>or inauspicious</gloss> or mixed, they are produced in their entirety by one who is skilled in the planetary periods <term>dasha</term> <note>planetary periods that govern specific times in a person's life</note>. Only then should the favorable results be considered, particularly when a benefic planet is involved. This should be deliberated through the consideration of the results of positional strength <term>sthana-bala</term>, directional strength <term>dig-bala</term>, and other such factors. Having stated the results of the planetary rays <note>referring to the previous page's topic</note>, the results arising from "Yogas" <term>yoga</term> <note>literally "union," referring to specific geometric alignments of planets</note> are now spoken of, as they are well-known in the traditional teachings and have various applications.

->**Now, the Sun Yogas**<-

When planets (excluding the Moon) are situated in the second house from the Sun, the **Vesi** yoga is formed. When they are in the twelfth house, it is **Vasi**. When planets are found in both the second and twelfth houses from the Sun, it is known as the **Ubhayachari** yoga. One should remember these. However, these do not apply if the planets involved are the Moon <note>original: "nishaprabho," refers to the Moon, which is excluded from forming these specific solar yogas</note>. A person born under a **Vesi** yoga is truthful, steady, and hardworking **1**. 

Regarding the vision of the Sunafa yoga <note>The scribe appears to be blending Solar and Lunar yoga descriptions here</note>: if one is born when there is a **Vesi** yoga, the person is virtuous, compassionate, and possesses a handsome appearance. They are celebrated and possess many children, yet they remain slightly humble despite their great prosperity **3**. If a person has the **Ubhayachari** yoga at birth, they will have a well-proportioned body, be extremely famous, and will not be ignorant. They will be endowed with many strong and praised children and will always remain wealthy **4**. 

When considering the strength of the Sun <term>Bhanu</term> in the northern or southern paths <note>referring to the solstices, Uttarayana and Dakshinayana</note>, the wise must contemplate these yogas. Truly, the fruits of these positions should be shared by all learned men after careful thought. If the **Vesi** yoga is present and well-placed, it destroys all misfortunes. Just as a fire of anger might be quenched, so too are the difficulties of life washed away by the flow of nectar-like results from these planetary alignments **8**.

->**Now, the Moon Yoga**<-

When a planet is placed in the second house from the Moon <term>Shitanshu</term>, the **Sunafa** yoga is born. If the planets are in the twelfth, it is **Anapha**. If they are in both, it is **Dhurdhara**. These should be known by the movements of the planets. If none of these are present <note>meaning no planets occupy the 2nd or 12th from the Moon</note>, it is known as the **Kemadruma** yoga, which is considered an unfavorable omen **3**. A person born with the **Sunafa** yoga is one who earns wealth through their own arms and is famous among people for their vast glory.

...the great joy born of Jupiter <note>original: "dhikapramadajaḥ"</note> in the **Sunapha** yoga <term>Sunapha</term> <note>a planetary combination where planets other than the Sun occupy the 2nd house from the Moon</note>: **8** The person is gentle, well-conducted, possesses the charm of auspicious speech, is virtuous and well-behaved, well-connected with people <gloss>or groups</gloss>, of great fame, firm in thought and memory, and a constant, steady man.

In the **Sunapha** yoga produced by Venus: **9** The person is honored and very wealthy, travels with vehicles, is joined with happiness, and is always a seeker of beauty and pleasure in the company of women; such people are steady and possess eternal courage. **10**

If produced by Saturn: The person is devoid of <gloss>good</gloss> children and family, becomes a servant, and lives in a foreign land; they are constantly of a contrary mind and wear dirty clothes. Even if the son of a lord of goats <note>a metaphor for a king or leader</note>, he becomes like one born under the **Kemadruma** yoga <term>Kemadruma</term> <note>an inauspicious yoga where no planets are in the 2nd or 12th houses from the Moon, signifying isolation or poverty</note>.

## Now, the results of Anapha
The results for **Anapha** <term>Anapha</term> <note>planets in the 12th house from the Moon</note>, **Sunapha**, and **Dhurdhura** <term>Dhurdhura</term> <note>planets in both the 2nd and 12th houses from the Moon</note> are now stated. If Mars is the cause, the person is very cruel and a wicked man. **12** If Mercury is the cause, the person is skilled, very wealthy, and honored by men. If Jupiter is the cause, the person is born into a royal family <gloss>or is like a king</gloss>, possesses many virtues, is devoted to righteousness <term>dharma</term>, and is filled with happiness. **13** If Venus is the cause, it brings an abundance of lordly power, fame among the people, and makes a man full of happiness. If Saturn is the cause, the person is a protector <gloss>or leader</gloss> of a group, though they may serve others at the door of the king.

## Now, the Cancellation of the Kemadruma Yoga
->**The breaking of the isolation**<-

If planets are situated in the angles <term>Kendra</term> <note>the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</note> from the Moon or the Ascendant, it makes the person knowledgeable, honored, skilled, and noble, even if the inauspicious yoga was present. **15** When the Moon is in a favorable sign or is aspected by all the planets, it produces a person who is wealthy and possesses a good treasury <note>original: "satkosha," meaning wealth or a library</note>, even if the **Kemadruma** yoga was indicated. **16** If all the planets are in the angles, it results in a "Camel Yoga" <note>original: "Ushtra Yoga," a specific configuration</note>; but if they are in the angles from the Moon, the **Kemadruma** is cancelled, and the results become wonderfully auspicious. **17** 

If the lord of the Moon's sign is in an angle, or if Jupiter is in the descendant, or if the Sun is in Virgo, or if Jupiter aspects the Moon, all these configurations destroy the remnants of the **Kemadruma** yoga. Kings and lords should consider this cancellation. **18**

### Now, the Water Union
### <note>original: "Atha Jala-yogaḥ"</note>
When the Sun, Moon, and Saturn <note>original: "Sūrya, Śaśāṅka, Manda"</note> are situated in the quadrants, or in the house of loss <note>the 12th house</note>, or in the houses of malefic planets, they create the "Water Union" <term>Jala-yoga</term>, provided the other planets possess "white strength" <note>likely referring to the brightness/digit-strength of the Moon</note> at that time. **9** 

One born of this yoga may be devoid of wealth, extremely restless or fickle, and constantly active. Such a person, though troubled, becomes like a king through the effects of the Water Union. **11**

### Now, the Isolation Unions

A person born under the **Kemadruma** yoga is restless and wanders. Even if they are born into a high family, they may become poor and addicted to the use of others' wealth, showing the various negative traits produced by this isolation. **12**

## The Alari Combination
<note>original: "lāri-prayoga"</note>

If all the planets are situated in their signs of debilitation <term>Neecha</term> <note>the sign where a planet is at its weakest</note>, or in the signs of their enemies, then even if a person is born into a high-status family <note>original: "dvijāti-garbhe," meaning a "twice-born" or priestly lineage</note>, they will be devoid of good deeds. Such a person will constantly wander as a beggar like an outcast <note>original: "Mleccha," a term used for foreigners or those outside the traditional social order</note>, having been born among low people. **16**

If the Lord of the Hour <term>Horadhipa</term> <note>the planet ruling the specific sign rising on the horizon at birth</note> is positioned in the Sun’s <unclear>radiance</unclear> and is a strong, cruel planet <note>a "malefic" like Mars or Saturn</note>, the person born under this combination will be sent to a foreign land and suffer great misfortune <term>Rishta</term>. **18**

If the auspicious planets <term>Shubha-grahas</term> are situated in the four quadrants <term>Kendra</term> and the house of wealth, and are not afflicted by the "sky-goers of sin" <note>malefic planets</note>, the person will be a conqueror of enemies. Such a man will be a leader who brings glory to his family. **17**

The wise ones have said, following the tradition of the Greeks <note>original: "Yavanenaiva," referring to the Ionian/Greek astrological influence in India</note>, that those born with the Sun and Moon in these specific degrees and placements will be seekers of knowledge and possessors of great effort. **18**

## The Combination of Low Conduct
<note>original: "Neechavrittiyoga"</note>

If, as mentioned before, the lord of the house of children <note>the 5th house</note> or the Ascendant is in <unclear>weakness</unclear> and the lord of that sign is placed in the house of loss <note>the 12th house</note>, the person born is declared to be one of low conduct. **19**

When the Lord of Fortune <term>Bhagyadhipa</term> <note>the planet ruling the 9th house of luck and spirituality</note> is in the house of children or the house of wealth, but is joined by malefic planets, the person will not live a life of high status but will fall into low habits. **21**

If the lords of the chart are weak, the person will be an ascetic or a beggar. **23** He will be scorched by constant worry and grief, lacking his own dignity and failing in his duties. **22**

If the Lord of the Stars <note>the Moon</note> and the Lord of Fortune are in each other’s houses, but are aspected by malefic planets, the person will lose their strength. **[no number]** When the Lord of Fortune is placed in the house of expenditure <note>the 12th house, representing loss</note> and is joined by malefic planets, the man will reside in a foreign land and will be deprived of his wife and children. **25**

If the lords of the Ascendant, the house of virtue, and the house of children are joined with Saturn <note>original: "Śani"</note> or Mars <note>original: "Āra"</note> in certain signs, the person born will constantly face obstacles in their efforts. **26**

## The Defiling Union

## The Family-Tainting Union
<note>original: "Kulapāṃsu-yoga"</note>

If the Lord of the Ascendant is in a quadrant, but the seventh house is occupied by malefics, or if the Lord of the Ascendant is viewed by planets of equal strength while the Benefics are weak and the Malefics are in the seventh house, this is called the Family-Tainting Union. **29**

The person born under this "Family-Tainter" combination will live in a foreign land, will always carry a staff in his hand <note>a sign of a wandering beggar or a manual laborer</note>, will be perpetually poor, and will lose both his children and his wife. Such a man will be overcome by a multitude of diseases. **30**

If the Ascendant is occupied by those two <note>likely referring to specific malefic planets mentioned in context</note> and they are in malefic signs, the man will be like a ghost <term>Pishacha</term> <note>a type of flesh-eating demon or malevolent spirit in Hindu mythology</note>. If the Sun is in the Ascendant and viewed by those same planets, he will be extremely agitated and distressed. **31**

If the Benefics and the Sun are in the Ascendant, or if they are in the trines <term>Trikona</term> <note>the 5th and 9th houses, considered very lucky</note>, and if the Lord of the house of wealth is in the twelfth house, the person will be... <unclear>deprived of radiance</unclear>. **32**

## The Union of Servitude
<note>original: "Preshya-yoga"</note>

If Saturn <term>Manda</term> is in the Ascendant and the Sun is in the seventh house, or if the Moon is in the third house and Jupiter is in a weak position, the person is born as a servant <term>Preshya</term>. There is no doubt about this; it cannot be otherwise. **33**

If the Sun and Moon are in the same sign, specifically in the division of the "Ape" <term>Vanara-bhaga</term> <note>a specific subsection of a zodiac sign</note>, or if the lords of the signs are in their signs of debilitation, the person will be born into a life of service. **34**

## Afflictions of the Body
<note>original: "Sharira-dosha"</note>

If Leo is the Ascendant and contains both the Sun and the Moon, the person will suffer from great diseases. If the Moon is viewed by both benefics and malefics, or if it is in the ninth or twelfth houses, the person will have a <unclear>distorted</unclear> form. **35**

If the cruel planets <term>Krura</term> <note>malefics like Mars or Saturn</note> are in the trines, or in the seventh or the Ascendant, and are viewed by other malefics, the man will surely suffer from chronic illnesses or be born with a physical disability. **36**

If a particular planetary arrangement occurs, it causes great suffering in the ears <note>hearing loss or pain</note> for the man. If the Moon is positioned there with Saturn, it creates <unclear>darkness</unclear> or deafness. **37**

...if the Sun and Moon are positioned in the twelfth house... **38**
