...if one should speak of the strength of the year or of the self-born <note>original: "ātmaja," referring to a son</note>. **39**

When malefic planets <term>Pāpagraha</term> <note>planets like Saturn, Mars, Rahu, and Ketu that typically bring challenges</note> are situated in houses associated with water or bricks <note>interpretive: likely referring to specific signs or architectural divisions of the chart</note>, they are not auspicious and are considered joined with misfortune. This is the cause of ailments arising from the seventh house <term>Jāmitra</term> <note>the house of partnerships and the lower abdomen</note>, specifically involving injuries to the navel. **40**

If the Sun <term>Dineśa</term> and the Moon <term>Śaśi</term> are placed in the third house at birth, or if the Lord of the Ascendant is in the third, the learned masters of the Rauru scriptures <note>original: "rauruvidbhiḥ," possibly referring to a specific school of astrological thought or lineage</note> declare certain results for the man. **41**

One should speak of the father, the self, the son, the brother, and the maternal uncle by observing the Lords of those respective houses. If they are joined with the Lord of the Ascendant or other specific markers, they may face obstructions or health challenges as described. **42**

## On Eye Disorders and Ulcers
<note>original: "Netra-vikāra"</note>

When cruel planets <term>Krūra</term> <note>malefics</note> are situated in the second house <note>the house governing the face and eyes</note> or in the "face of the horse" <note>an astrological sub-division</note>, the person will be afflicted by eye diseases. If these planets are associated with the eyes <note>the 2nd and 12th houses</note>, an ulcer or a wound may occur. If the son of the Sun <term>Savitā-ātmaja</term> <note>Saturn</note> is placed there, the person will suffer from chronic pain. **43**

If the son of Simhika <term>Simhī-suta</term> <note>Rahu, the North Node of the Moon</note> is in the Ascendant and the Moon is joined with it, the person will suffer from a "yellow defect" <note>original: "pīta-doṣa," likely jaundice or a bile/Pitta imbalance</note>. Such a man will also be prone to falls or accidents. **44**

## On Abdominal Ailments and the Spleen
<note>original: "Gulma-roga" and "Plīha"</note>

If the Sun and Saturn <term>Arka-sūnu</term> are in the house of passion <note>the 7th house</note>, the man will suffer from diseases of the spleen <term>Plīha</term>, abdominal tumors or lumps <term>Gulma</term>, and jaundice. Such a person may also suffer from abscesses <term>Vidradhi</term>. **45**

If the Moon is in the sixth or eighth house and is preceded by Venus, or if Saturn and Mars <term>Anala</term> are present, the person will suffer from "speedy" diseases <note>original: "vegada-roga," possibly referring to acute conditions or those affecting excretory functions</note>. If Mars <term>Sāra</term> and the Moon are in the Ascendant, the digestive fire becomes "inverted" <note>original: "viloma-vahni," meaning poor digestion or acid reflux</note>, leading to various sicknesses. **46**

If the Moon is in the signs of the Crab <note>Cancer</note> or the Insect <note>Scorpio</note> and is joined by malefic planets, the person will be afflicted by secret or genital diseases <term>Guhya-roga</term>. If Saturn <term>Sūrya-suta</term> is in the second house and Mars is in the twelfth, the man will have a weak or wasted body. **47**

If Saturn and the Sun are in Gemini, or if they have exchanged their planetary portions <note>an exchange of signs known as Parivartana Yoga</note>, they cause "wasting heat" <note>original: "śoṣa-tapantau," referring to tuberculosis or chronic wasting fever</note> in men. Likewise, this leads to chronic digestive illnesses. **48**

If Mars is in the eleventh house <note>the house of gains</note> and Saturn is in the house of death <note>the 8th house</note>, the person will be afflicted by pain caused by malefic influences. If the Sun is in the seventh house, and the Sun and Saturn are in specific combinations, it leads to the breaking of limbs and blood-related disorders. **49**

<note>The manuscript repeats the number 49 for the following verse</note>

If Saturn and Rahu are in the eighth house, men will suffer from the pain of diarrhea and dysentery <term>Atīsāra</term>. This occurs even if they are in the house of sorrow or the house of waste. If the Sun is in the seventh... **49**

If the sign Cancer <term>Karka</term> <note>the fourth sign of the zodiac, associated with water</note> is rising at birth, and the Sun and Saturn are positioned in watery signs, and the Lord of the 10th house <term>Vyomeśvara</term> is joined with Saturn, the man will be devoid of virility. **50**

Such a man will also be afflicted by chronic urinary diseases or metabolic disorders <term>Prameha</term> <note>original: "pramehāmayam," a category of 20 diseases in Ayurveda, often including what we now call diabetes</note>. **50** 

If the Sun is in the seventh house <term>Jāmitra</term> <note>the house governing partnerships and the lower abdomen</note> along with malefic planets, the person will suffer from an enlargement of the abdomen caused by wind <term>Vātodara</term> <note>a condition characterized by gas, bloating, or abdominal tumors in traditional medicine</note> or be tormented by skin diseases. **51**

When Mars <term>Mahīja</term> is in the trines <term>Trikoṇa</term> <note>the 1st, 5th, or 9th houses</note> or the 7th house, and the Sun or Saturn are similarly placed, or if Saturn and the Sun are in the 12th house <term>Vyaya</term>, there will be a significant and constant defect in the person's eyesight. **52**

If the Ascendant <term>Lagna</term> is in Taurus or Aries and is occupied by cruel planets <term>Krūra</term> <note>malefics like Saturn or Mars</note>, the person will have deformed or diseased teeth. If the Sun is in certain positions in the 2nd house <term>Vitta</term>, similar dental issues arise. **53**

If the 5th or 9th houses <term>Dhī-dharma</term> are occupied by non-benefic planets, the man will be slow of sight <note>original: "manda-vilocana," meaning poor vision or blindness</note>. If Mars <term>Bhūmisuta</term> is in certain signs, he may suffer from diseases of the head or ears. **54**

## **Now, the Combination for Imprisonment**
<note>original: "Atha bandhanayogaḥ"</note>

If malefic planets <term>Asaumya</term> are situated in the 12th house, the trines, or the 2nd house <term>Artha</term>, the human being will suffer bondage. If the Ascendant <term>Lagna</term> is in Sagittarius, Aries, or Taurus, the binding will be like that of a rope <term>Rajju</term>. **55**

If the Ascendant is Gemini, Virgo, Libra, or Aquarius, the bondage arises from shackles or fetters <term>Nigaḍa</term>. If the Ascendant is in Cancer, Leo, Pisces, or Capricorn, the person may be bound by wooden stocks <term>Śṛṅga</term> or chains <term>Jañga</term>. **56**

## **Now, the Combination for the Consumer**
<note>original: "Atha bhakṣakayogaḥ"</note>

When the 10th house <term>Karma</term> is occupied by planets devoid of any benefic aspect <term>Śubha-dṛṣṭi</term>, and certain malefic signs are present, the sages declare the person to be a "consumer"—one who is either gluttonous or destined to consume their own resources through bad habits. **57**

## **Now, the Combination for Leprosy**
<note>original: "Atha kuṣṭhayogaḥ"</note>

If the Moon <term>Vidhu</term> is situated between malefic planets in Aries or Scorpio, or is aspected by Mars, or if the Moon is in Aries, Capricorn, or Cancer, the man will become a sufferer of leprosy <term>Kuṣṭhavān</term> <note>the term "kushtha" in ancient texts covers a broad range of severe skin conditions, including modern leprosy and psoriasis</note>. **58**

If the 5th or 9th houses <term>Dhī-dharma</term> are joined with or aspected by cruel planets, there may be pain or white leprosy <term>Śveta-kuṣṭha</term> <note>likely referring to vitiligo or leucoderma</note> on the body. **59**

## **Now, the Combination for Epilepsy**
<note>original: "Athāpasmārayogaḥ"</note>

If the Sun and other malefics are in certain positions and aspect the body, the sages say the man will suffer from the affliction known as epilepsy <term>Apasmāra</term> <note>original: "apasmārajāto," literally "born of forgetfulness," the traditional term for falling sickness or seizures</note>. **60**

If malefics are in the quadrants <term>Kendra</term> or the 8th house <term>Vināśa</term>, and the Moon and Sun are in the 7th, the person born under this combination will suffer from epilepsy.

### Now, the Combination for Deformity
#### <note>original: "Atha Kunja-dosha-yoga"</note>

If the Lord of the Ascendant <term>Lagnādhipati</term> is weak and positioned between Mars <term>Kuja</term> and Saturn <term>Manda</term>, or if Mars is placed in the heart <note>referring to the 4th house or the chest area of the cosmic person</note> of the birth chart, the person born will be a "Kunja" <note>original: "kuñjaḥ"; historically referring to a person with a physical deformity, often a hunchback or someone of stunted growth</note>. **72**

### Now, the Combinations for Skin Diseases
#### <note>original: "Atha Kuṣṭha-yoga"</note>

If the Moon <term>Candra</term> is joined with Mars <term>Vani</term> and Saturn <term>Asita</term> in the sign of the Sun <note>Leo</note>, and if these are aspected by malefic planets <term>Krūra</term>, the person will certainly suffer from chronic skin diseases or leprosy <term>Kuṣṭha</term> <note>original: "kuṣṭhabhavedvraṇānāṃ"; in Ayurveda, *kushtha* is a broad term for various skin pathologies, including leprosy and severe psoriasis</note> accompanied by ulcers <term>Vraṇa</term>. **74**

If Venus <term>Sita</term> is aspected by malefic planets, the person may suffer from "white leprosy" <note>original: "sragdhapa"; likely referring to Vitiligo or leucoderma</note>. If Mars <term>Bhūmiputra</term> and Saturn <term>Arki</term> are in a specific combination and lack the aspect of benefic planets, the person will suffer from scrofula <term>Gaṇḍamālā</term> <note>swelling of the lymph nodes in the neck</note> and persistent ulcers. **75**

When the Moon <term>Candra</term> is in the Ascendant and joined with the Lord of the 8th house, it is said that leprosy <term>Kushtha</term> will manifest. If the Moon is in a malefic house and aspected by Saturn <term>Shani</term>, the disease will be severe and cause great distress. **76**

If Mars <term>Kṣitija</term> is aspected by Jupiter <term>Deva-ejya</term>, this may be mitigated, but if Mars is destroyed <note>weakened by position</note>, the person will suffer from ailments in the genitals <term>Linga</term> or in other parts of the body. **79**

If malefic planets are in the 7th house and the Lord of the Ascendant is with Saturn <term>Sauri</term>, the person's wife will be sickly or the person will suffer from "Kusmayate" <note>unclear; likely a fever or wasting condition</note> at night. **80**

### Now, the Combination for Impotence
#### <note>original: "Atha Shadatva-yoga"</note>

### Now, the Combination for Overwhelming Lust

### Now, the Combination for Hemorrhoids
#### <note>original: "Atha Ashilobha"</note>

If malefic planets <term>Krūra</term> are in the seventh house and the lord of the seventh is aspected by benefic planets, there will be a partial ailment of the seat. However, if the lord of the eighth house <term>Randhresha</term> is joined with Saturn <term>Manda</term> or if Mars is in the seventh house, the person will suffer from hemorrhoids <term>Ashilobha</term> <note>original: "ashilobha"; often a variant of *arshas*, the Ayurvedic term for piles or hemorrhoids</note>. **88** <note>The manuscript numbering appears to repeat or fluctuate here.</note>

If Saturn <term>Manda</term> is in the eighth house and the Lord of the Ascendant is joined with Rahu <note>the North Node of the Moon</note>, or if the Sun is aspected by them, or if Mars <term>Bhauma</term> is in the Ascendant, the person will suffer from chronic pain or disorders of the secret parts. **85**

### Now, the Combination for Ulcers
#### <note>original: "Atha Vranaroga"</note>

If the Lord of the Ascendant <term>Lagna</term> is in the sign of Aries <note>the head of the cosmic man</note> and is joined with Jupiter <term>Guru</term>, or if Saturn <term>Ravija</term> is in a specific division, the person will suffer from sores or ulcers <term>Vraṇa</term> caused by external accidents or internal heat. If malefic planets are in the seventh house, there will be wounds on the body. **50**

### Now, the Combination for Blood Disorders
#### <note>original: "Atha Raktaroga"</note>

It is said that if Rahu <term>Rāhupo</term> is in Aries <term>Mesha</term>, Taurus <term>Vrishabha</term>, or Virgo <term>Kanyā</term>, and is joined with malefic planets, the person will suffer from disorders of the blood <term>Raktaroga</term>. This may manifest as swelling, rashes, or exhaustion <term>Māndya</term>. **51**

If the lord of the sixth house <term>Rogādhipati</term> is in a female sign and aspected by Venus <term>Bhārgava</term> or Mars <term>Bhūmi</term>, there will be an increase in boils or eruptions <term>Phoda-vṛddhi</term> on the skin. **52**

### Now, the Combination for Stunted Growth
#### <note>original: "Atha Vamana Dosha"</note>

If the Lord of the Ascendant is in the very last degrees of a sign, or if the Moon and the Sun are in specific weak positions, the person will be a "Vamana" <note>original: "vāmana"; a person of short stature or a dwarf</note>.

Now, the **affliction of baldness** <term>Khalyāṭa-dosha</term>: if the Ascendant is Leo, Scorpio, or Virgo, or if the signs of Cancer, Taurus, or Aries are aspected by Saturn <note>original: "Arkaputra," literally "Son of the Sun"</note>, the person will have a bald head. **54**

Now, the **affliction of being mute or deaf** <term>Mūka-vadhira</term>: if the Moon is located in the decanates <term>Drekkāna</term> of Aries, or if the Moon occupies the twelfth or second house from the Ascendant, such a person will be born with these impairments. **55**

Now, the **affliction of bodily luster** <term>Deha-kānti-dosha</term>: if the Moon <term>Shashānka</term> and Saturn are joined in the sign of Aries, or if they are situated in a cardinal house <term>Kendra</term> without the aspect of a benefic planet, the body will become emaciated and sickly. **56**

Thus ends the **Chapter on Bodily Defects** in the "Refined Astrology" <note>original: "Shodha Hora"</note>.

# Now, the Chapter on Livelihood
### <note>original: "Atha Jīvikādhyāyaḥ"</note>

A person's livelihood <term>Ājīvika</term> is determined by the planet occupying the tenth house <term>Karma</term> from the Ascendant, the Moon, or the Sun. From the strongest of these three, the source of wealth must be predicted. **1**

If the Sun is the source of strength, one earns through the father. If the Sun is in the tenth house, the person may earn through their own power, through the king <note>original: "Nṛpa"; in a modern context, this refers to government or high authority</note>, or through the advice of kin. The nature of the work is determined by the sign the Sun occupies. **4**

One earns through the sale of auspicious items, through the knowledge of the scriptures <term>Shāstra</term>, or through the favor of a king if the Sun is in a powerful division. **5**

If the Moon is the source, one gains wealth through pearls, gems, agriculture, or activities involving the sea. Wealth may also be acquired through the influence of women <note>original: "strī-prasaṅga"</note>, or through trade and gift-giving. **6**

If Mars <term>Bhauma</term> is the source, one gains wealth through teaching skills, the use of weapons, fire-related work, or medicine <term>Bhishaja</term>. Livelihood may also come from physical prowess, acts of courage, or working with metal. **7**

If Mercury <term>Vid</term> is the source, one earns through plowing <note>unclear: "halisha"</note>, poetry, arts, education, one's own speech, or through mathematics <term>Gaṇita</term>...

<summary>This page concludes the discussion on physical ailments (dwarfism, baldness, and muteness) and begins the "Chapter on Livelihood," detailing how planets in the tenth house indicate a person's profession and sources of wealth.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the "Chapter on Livelihood" from the previous page, detailing the specific sources of income associated with Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn. It concludes this chapter and begins a new technical section on the "Seven Divisions" (Saptavarga) of planetary strength.</meta>

जो <note>original: "Jo," likely an abbreviation for "Jyotisha" (Astrology) or "Jātaka" (Nativity)</note>

...[wealth] is gained through the intellect and the performance of good deeds. A wise person always obtains the fruit of their actions. **8**

Through the worship of deities, the study of scriptures, and the recitation of sacred mantras <term>Mantra-japa</term>, wealth is acquired. The wise also gain through the favor of kings, from scholars, or through the various arts. This is how the noble person finds their fortune. **9**

Through horses, elephants, and buffaloes; from women, jewelry, and gold; and through mastery in various skills of singing and dancing, one always acquires wealth when Venus <note>original: "Sita," a name for the planet Venus meaning "the white one"</note> is the source of influence. **10**

Through the "Son of the Sun" <note>original: "Suryatmaja," referring to the planet Saturn</note>, a person earns through fruits, roots, and leaves; from laboring for low-status individuals; or through trade and commerce, even if it involves dishonesty. Thus, a man gains his wealth. **11**

If the planet is in its highest strength or a superior sign, the full results described above are experienced with happiness. However, if the planet is in its sign of debilitation <term>Nīcha</term>, the result is very small; if the planetary positions are varied, the results will be mixed. **12**

The direction of the zodiac signs and their natural tendencies must be considered. According to the lords of the signs and their aspects, the nature of the results should be determined. **13**

If the planet or its lord is in its own sign or aspects it, the sages declare that the results will manifest in one's own country. Otherwise, if influenced by other factors, the result may manifest elsewhere. **14**

If the planet is in the final degrees of a sign, wealth may come through travel, water-related journeys, or residing in foreign lands. If the signs are fixed <term>Sthira</term>, the status remains stable in one's own country; otherwise, there may be fear or instability. **15**

If the lord of the house is retrograde <term>Vakra</term>, wealth is attained through various unconventional means. Scholars of the stars <term>Tantu-vid</term> should indicate the person's destiny and fortune from the very beginning. ... **16**

...Fame and success are generated through one's children or through the strength of the Ascendant <term>Lagna</term>. **17**

**Thus ends the Chapter on Livelihood.**

# Now, the Determination of Results for the Seven Divisions of the Planets
### <note>original: "Atha Grahāṇāṃ Saptavarga Phala Nirṇayaḥ"</note>

The results of the planets are spoken of according to the portion of the zodiac <term>Ansha</term> they occupy. Whether in the trines <term>Trikona</term> or the six divisions <term>Shadvarga</term>, the wise should examine the planet. If a single planet is the ruler of the earth... <note>The text here becomes technical, discussing the "Universal Ruler" (Sarvabhauma) combination where a planet dominates several of the seven vargas or divisional charts.</note>

...[acquired] through mantras and the influence of planets. **18** Auspicious results come from planets in their own signs, friends' signs, or signs of exaltation <term>Ucca</term>; inauspicious results arise from signs of enemies or debilitation <term>Nīcha</term>. Thus, one should examine both types of fruits and weigh them against each other. **19** When the auspicious divisions <term>Vargas</term> are more numerous, even a "cruel" <note>original: "Krura," meaning malefic</note> planet becomes auspicious and a gentle planet becomes exceedingly beautiful in its effects. If the lower [debilitated] divisions are greater, a "good" planet may act harshly, and a "cruel" one will act with even greater cruelty. **20**

### Thus ends the chapter on the results of the conjunction of two planets.

### Now begins the chapter on the conjunction of three planets.

Through the three <note>planets</note>, one becomes like an arrow in the ritual of the gods... a son of the day <note>the Sun</note>. A man will be like a lotus <term>pankaja</term> to be served if he is born under the combination of the Sun <term>Ashitadyuti</term>, Mars <term>Vakra</term>, and the Moon <term>Chandra</term>. He will be a doer of good deeds. If Mars is retrograde at the time of birth, he becomes a king... 

<margin>1</margin> He is delighted by the arts of the aesthetic sciences <term>kala-shastra</term>. This yoga <note>combination</note> is known by the name "Nectar-handed" <term>Amrita-kara</term>. If this occurs at the time of birth, the man remains under its influence. **1** 

<margin>4</margin> He may be restless, but a master of the lute <term>vīṇā</term>; an expert in service and one who travels to foreign lands. It is said that this yoga surely brings a position of authority over ships <note>or "commands over water"</note> for men at the time of birth. 

<margin>5</margin> The person will have an interest in social gatherings. Like a lotus-pond <term>sarojini</term> or the color of a lotus, he is dressed in fine garments... if the son is born under this influence. 
His mind is steady and fixed on the highest goal <term>Me-ro</term>, and he is constantly engaged in the rituals of knowledge with great fortitude. If a man is born under this unique combination, he will be excellent. 
He will feel a sense of modesty born from his nature and will be a friend to women. He will be of virtuous character, with a well-settled intellect and famous. He will be an expert in the various methods of assembly when Jupiter and Mercury are joined. 
Through the influence of the son of the Moon <note>Mercury</note>, he becomes wealthy. Under the influence of Mars <term>Vakra</term>, he becomes a minister to kings, possessing great wealth. His life will be full of luxury and he will be an honorable man, through the conjunction of Jupiter and Venus. **3** He will be endowed with enjoyments, a master of the treasury and the sciences, humble, and highly clever in his conduct.

A person will be a good friend, possess widespread fame, and have a well-proportioned body when the Moon <term>Shashi</term>, Mercury <term>Saumya</term>, and Jupiter <term>Jiva</term> are situated together in a single house. **20**

One becomes exceptionally proficient in learning and wisdom when joined with Jupiter <term>Brihaspati</term>. If Mercury <term>Jna</term>, Saturn <term>Saura</term>, and Jupiter <term>Arya</term> are positioned together at the time of birth, the person is highly intelligent; otherwise, if they are placed differently, the results vary. **21**

Famous, well-regarded, and serving as a leader or an official in a village <note>original: "pragrāmakṛtādhikāraḥ," implying local administrative authority</note>, such an individual possesses a pure intellect and is skilled in the various arts. This occurs when Mercury <term>Jna</term> is joined with the Moon <term>Soma</term>; such a person may also become an ascetic <term>yati</term>. **22**

One will be exceedingly fortunate, endowed with good qualities and fame, and possessed of a righteous intellect and strength if the Moon <term>Shashi</term>, Jupiter <term>Jiva</term>, and Mercury <term>Saumya</term> are joined together at the time of birth. Such a person may also lean toward the life of an ascetic. **23**

He becomes authorized in the three types of scriptures <term>shastras</term>, a servant of the king, and very wise, provided that Mars <term>Hauja</term> is not aspected by Saturn <term>Shanaishchara</term> in this planetary combination. **24**

Liked by others, possessing excellent books and wealth, and skilled in writing, this person is devoted to meritorious deeds, an expert in the Vedas <term>shruti</term>, and highly discerning. This is the result when Mars <term>Uja</term>, Mercury <term>Jna</term>, and Jupiter <term>Girisa</term> <note>original: "girisa," a name for Shiva used here to denote Jupiter</note> are in conjunction. **25**

Talkative, restless, thin-bodied, and constantly energetic, such a man may be separated from his kin. He might serve as a messenger for a king and becomes an ascetic if Mars <term>Uja</term>, Mercury <term>Jna</term>, and Venus <term>Daiteyaja</term> <note>literally "son of Diti," a common epithet for Venus in astrological texts</note> are joined. **26**

Filled with fear, possessing a wasted body, serving others, and living as a traveler with afflicted eyes <note>original: "shruvilochana," referring to weak or watery eyesight</note>—this is the difficult yoga formed when Mars <term>Uja</term>, Mercury <term>Jna</term>, and the Sun <term>Arka</term> are together at birth. **27**

Endowed with the happiness of a wife and sons, honored by kings, and a follower of virtuous people—this describes the man born when Mars <term>Avanija</term>, Jupiter <term>Amarejya</term>, and the Moon <term>Soma</term> are joined. **28**

Disrespected by kings, devoid of mercy, lean, and possessing a cruel temperament without the happiness of friends—such is the man born when Mars <term>Lohitanga</term>, Jupiter <term>Girisa</term>, and Saturn <term>Arkasuta</term> <note>literally "son of the Sun"</note> reside in a single house. **29**

Living in a foreign land and bringing misfortune to his mother, such an individual is an unsteady leader <note>original: "uvanganeśro"</note> when the Moon <term>Soma</term>, Mars <term>Heti</term>, and the son of the Sun, Saturn <term>Arkatanaya</term>, are joined at the time of birth. **30**

Possessing courage and a voice for bold songs...

...a person becomes a conqueror of his enemies' sons, possessed of a virtuous character and form, when born under the conjunction of the Sun <note>original: "vṛṣa" is used here, likely a variant or specific astrological reference to the Sun or the sign Taurus</note>, Jupiter <term>Jiva</term>, and Venus <term>Shukra</term> situated in a single house. Because of this combination, even if the person lacks initial happiness, they will eventually be joined with the qualities of goodness <term>sattva-guna</term>. If Mercury <term>Budha</term> and Jupiter <term>Jiva</term> are specifically placed at the time of birth, the individual possesses infinite wisdom. **12**

One might be a speaker of untruths, fierce, or perhaps an outcast who has abandoned good conduct, if the birth occurs under a yoga <note>a planetary alignment</note> of the Sun <term>Arka</term>, Mercury <term>Budha</term>, and Venus <term>Shukra</term>, depending on their specific artistic divisions <term>kala</term> and vows. **13**

Even if a man is born into a low position or is otherwise unremarkable, he becomes a lord of the earth <term>Prithivipati</term> <note>a king or high ruler</note> if these three planets—Mercury, Venus, and the Sun—are positioned in their bright and powerful degrees at the time of birth. **14**

Thus ends the section on the effects of Three-Planet Conjunctions <term>Tri-graha Yoga</term>. If these planets are joined with malefic ones, they may cause the destruction of the lineage; however, if they are well-placed, they bring great dignity and honor from kings, making the individual the best among their kin. **15**

### Now, the Royal Combinations <term>Raja Yoga</term>

Having explained the results of the house lords, I shall now describe in detail the Royal Combinations <term>Raja Yogas</term>, which bring about the birth of a ruler. I speak of these for the benefit of all people. **1**

If five heavenly bodies <term>khechara</term> <note>literally "sky-goers," referring to the planets</note> are situated in their signs of exaltation <term>ucca</term> at the time of birth, the person becomes a capable universal emperor <term>Sarvabhauma</term>. If three planets reach their exaltation, the person becomes a king <term>Nrupala</term>; if fewer, they may be the son of a king or a powerful minister. **2**

A person born when four planets have reached their exaltation will have their fame spread across the surface of the earth, reaching as far as the high mountains and the great bridge of the ocean <note>original: "Mahasetu," likely a reference to Rama’s Bridge/Adam's Bridge, symbolizing the southern extent of the world</note>. **3**

Through the influence of Mars <term>Bhauma</term>, Jupiter <term>Jiva</term>, and Saturn <term>Shani</term>, various types of power are attained. There are sixteen specific variations of these yogas to be remembered. Even if a single planet—the Moon <term>Shitagu</term>—is involved in a specific way with the others, it can result in these sixteen outcomes. **4**

When the Moon <term>Himakara</term> is in the best divisional sign <term>Vargottama</term> and is aspected by four or more planets, thirty-two types of royal status are possible. Such a yoga allows a king to rule the earth as far as the boundaries of the oceans. **5**

If **Venus** <term>sita</term> is with **Saturn** <term>asitah</term> in the **tenth house** <term>chaite</term>, and the **Son of the Lotus-dweller** <note>original: "sarojini sutas," likely referring to Mercury or a specific planetary alignment</note> is in a **watery sign** <term>jale charai</term> or in **Taurus** <term>gavi</term>, then he becomes a **king** <term>nripah</term>. **61**

When a **planet** <term>khecharo</term> occupies the second sign from its own, or if a **leader of kings** <term>nripanayakah</term> is situated in a **quadrant** <term>kendragau</term> in a **dual sign** <term>dvibhavanah</term>, it is said by the sages that a **king is born** <term>nripati sambhava</term>. **62**
<margin>73</margin>
When **Saturn** <term>samirano</term> <note>literally "the wind," here used for the planet Saturn</note> is in the signs of **Mars** <term>anala</term> and **Jupiter** <term>jiva</term>, and the **minister of the king of gods** <note>original: "danujendra mantri," referring to Jupiter</note> is present at birth, he makes the man a **sovereign** <term>naradhipatyam</term> born of a **royal lineage** <term>nripavamshajatam</term>. **63**

The **attainment of the kingdom** <term>rajyopalabdhir</term> occurs during the **planetary period** <term>dasha</term> of the planet situated in the **tenth house** <term>karmasthasya</term> or the **Ascendant** <term>vilagna</term>, provided that planet is endowed with **strength** <term>viryanvitasya</term> at the **time of birth** <term>prasuti kale</term>. **64**

---

### Thus ends the Chapter on Royal Yogas in the *Shambhu Hora Prakasha* composed by the astrologer Punjraja.

---

One should know that at the time of birth, powerful **royal combinations** <term>nripala-yoga</term> occur. If a man is born with the **auspicious mark of a conch** <term>sashankha lanchhana</term>, it is a sign of excellence. When such a mark appears on the **palm of the hand** <term>panitale</term>, it indicates great destiny. <margin>at the base of the ring finger</margin>
When a **line** <term>rekha</term> at the base of the **ring finger** <term>anamika</term> is shaped like a **lotus** <term>padma</term> and reaches the middle of the hand, one should know that it grants a kingdom. **1**

When the **twin physicians** <note>original: "ashvinau," referring to the Ashvini Kumaras, here likely a specific astrological or palmistry alignment</note> are graced by **benefic planets** <term>shubhagrahai</term>, the person will be **disciplined** <term>vinitah</term>, **famous** <term>yashasvi</term>, and adorned with **beautiful garments and jewelry**. **3**

If the sign of a **mountain** <note>original: "vaisagiri"</note>, an **elephant** <term>varano</term>, a **lute** <term>veena</term>, or a **crown** <term>mukutini</term> is found on the **hand** <term>panau</term>, that excellent man is a **king** <term>nripa</term> and a **lord of men** <term>naradhipah</term>. **4**

Signs of a **sword** <term>karavala</term>, a **garland** <term>mala</term>, or a **lotus petal** <term>dala padma</term>, or a **horse** <term>vaji</term> on the hand make one a **lord of the people** <term>natha</term>, or a **provincial ruler** <term>mandallika</term>, or the head of a **great clan** <term>kulam</term>. **5**

And if the sign of an **elephant** <term>varano</term> or a **victorious fly-whisk** <note>original: "vyajan," a traditional royal fan</note> is present, the goddess of fortune, **Lakshmi** <term>kamala</term>, <margin>4.</margin> will surely reside with that man.

...with elegant movements and a graceful disposition, it is said by the lineage of sages born of the mountains <note>likely referring to Agastya or other mountain-dwelling seers</note> that among the assembly of the immortals: if the sign of a **pillar** <term>stambha</term> or a **water-pot** <term>kumbha</term> is found on the feet, or if a **staff** <term>danda</term> or a **horse** <term>ghotaka</term> appears on the palm of the hand, that man shines as the most excellent of mortals. Through these **marks of royalty** <term>rajalakshana</term> explained here, kings are recognized by their dress and appearance. **7/9** <note>The numbering in the manuscript appears to fluctuate between verse counts</note>

He whose eyes are wide, reaching toward the ears like the petals of a **blue lotus** <term>nilotpala</term>, whose head is well-rounded, and who possesses the signs of the **goad** <term>ankusha</term> and the **lotus** <term>amburuha</term>, will see the destruction of his enemies. **8**

His nails and fingers are well-proportioned, appearing as if they hold the circle of the earth. His nose is straight and well-formed, resembling a smooth stone slab. **5** 

If the **navel** <term>nabhi</term> is deep, and he possesses these specific marks on his voice and gait, he becomes a **King of Kings** <term>rajarajah</term>. Of this, there is no doubt. **10**

The man who takes pride in his lineage, who is a knower of all things, who possesses a serene countenance, and who bows to **gurus and holy men** <term>guru-sadhu</term>; he who fears injustice and possesses the charm of auspicious speech, such a person attains the **Fortune of Sovereignty** <note>original: "Rajyalakshmi," the goddess of the kingdom's prosperity</note>. **11**

A **mole** <term>tila</term> on the palm of the hand indicates the arrival of wealth, though it may be infrequent. A mole on the foot indicates that the person will be a lord, but one who is constantly traveling or leading an army. **12**

The wise say that these signs belong to those born in royal houses. For those born into other families, these marks bear only a small portion of their fruit, perhaps making them a **minister** <term>mantri</term> or a leader within their own kin. **13**

These signs, as established by the ancient sages, provide their full results when they are clearly visible on the teeth, hands, and feet of men. If other signs are present instead, they may only bring moderate happiness or specific visual qualities. **14**

**Thus ends the Chapter on Physiognomy <term>Samudrikadhyaya</term> in the Auspicious *Great Light of Radiance* <term>Pradyotogra-prakasha</term>.**

Now, the discussion turns to those born under auspicious planets in high or low positions. We shall speak of the **Royal Unions** <term>rajayoga</term> that exist at the **time of birth** <term>sutikale</term>. This should be explained clearly by the learned. **1**

When the lord of the birth star <term>nakshatreshah</term> or the lord of the rising sign <term>lagnesha</term> is not weakened, and a planet at the time of birth is positioned as described, the Royal Union is heard. If these are not present, the yoga is broken. **2**

If the **Lord of the Night** <note>original: "yamini-ishe," the Moon</note> is in its own sign, untouched by "cruel" planets <note>original: "papai," referring to Mars, Saturn, or Rahu</note> and aspected by "gentle" planets <note>original: "saumya," referring to Jupiter, Venus, or Mercury</note>, the mortal attains the radiance of kingship. However, if afflicted, he may later encounter sorrow like one consumed by fire. **3**

Even if one is born as a daughter of a king <term>rajaputryage</term>, if birth occurs during the **Vyatipata** <note>an inauspicious astronomical yoga</note> on a day of great portents...

In such a case, even the "Arrow of God" combination only manages to maintain the strength of the royal yoga through great effort.

<margin>Man/King 4</margin> Nor is there a lack of poverty <note>meaning, poverty is likely</note>. **5** If at the time of birth, the **son of Bhrigu** <term>Venus</term> is in its position of deepest debilitation <note>the sign of Virgo</note>, even a person born of noble lineage becomes lowly and loses their inheritance. **6** If the **Teacher of the Gods** <term>Jupiter</term> is in its sign of debilitation while another planet is in its exaltation in front of it, the resulting combination is complex; wise men say that not even one of the royal yogas will bear fruit for such a person. **7**

When both **Jupiter and Venus** are in their signs of debilitation, or if all planets are situated in the **angles** <term>Kendras; the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</term> but the auspicious planets are placed in the **house of loss** <term>the 12th house</term>, then all royal combinations become useless at that time. **8**

If the **Moon** <term>Chandra</term> is under the influence of the house of a priest <note>Jupiter's signs</note> and is joined with **Mars** <term>Lohitanga</term> and the **Sun** <term>Arka</term>, but the auspicious planets are weak and the angles are vacant, one should know that the true nature of the royal yoga is lost. When the planets are devoid of strength or are eclipsed by the Sun, even if they are in their signs of debilitation or enemy signs, the person will not attain the status of a protector of the earth <note>a king</note>, and all such yogas pass away without result. **9**

### Thus ends the Chapter on the Cancellation of Royal Combinations in the Illustrious Shambhu Hora Prakasha.

### Now begin the Characteristics of the Five Great Men.

There are five types of kings, known as the "Great Men," who have been described by the ancient sages of old. I shall now explain them in their proper order, according to their strength and purity, as they are established by the rules of royal yogas. **1** These yogas occur when **Mars** <term>Kuja</term> and the other planets are strong and placed in the **angles** <term>Kendras</term> in their own signs or exaltation signs. In order, these are known as the **Ruchaka, Bhadra, Malavya, Hamsa, and Shasha** yogas. **2**

A man born under the **Ruchaka** yoga <note>governed by Mars</note> has a darkish or "smoky" complexion; he is courageous, with blue-black hair and great valor. He is distinct in his intelligence, possesses a neck shaped like a **conch shell** <term>kambu-kantha</term>, and has powerful knees and a handsome form. He is known for his success and is highly respected. **3** He is a devotee of the learned, a leader of many, and a master of mystic spells <term>mantras</term>. He possesses the strength to overcome his enemies, has a radiant and shifting luster, and wide renown. His hands and feet are marked with the signs of the **lotus** and the **serpent**. **4** He is a master of the **sciences** <term>Shastras</term>, the bow, and traditional lore. His hands and feet are marked by the signs of the sword and the kettle-drum. He is slightly prone to anger and is skilled in the use of mechanical devices and mystic charms...

<summary>This page concludes the discussion on planetary alignments that cancel out royal fortunes (Rajayoga-bhanga) and introduces the "Pancha Mahapurusha" yogas, detailing the physical and character traits of the Ruchaka type.</summary>
<keywords>Shambhu Hora Prakasha, Rajayoga, planetary debilitation, Pancha Mahapurusha, Ruchaka Yoga, Mars, astrological physiology, Vedic astrology</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "Five Great Men" (Pancha Mahapurusha) yogas from a manuscript on Samudrika Shastra (physiognomy and body omens). ...is not equal to any other. **5** The one born with these traits, known as the **Ruchaka** <note>original: "ruchakabhiyane"; a type of "Great Man" yoga caused by a strong Mars</note>, may meet his end near a temple, or through the destruction caused by weapons or fire. **6**

**Thus ends the Ruchaka type.**

Now, regarding the characteristics of the **Bhadra** <note>a "Great Man" yoga caused by a strong Mercury</note> type: He possesses the majesty of a tiger and the gait of an elephant. His thighs and chest are broad and fleshy. His arms are long and thick, reaching toward his knees. He is a master of yoga and possesses great vital strength. **7**

He bears the marks of a <term>conch, sword, elephant-goad, and discus</term> <note>original: "shangkhamisunjjagarakangkasamechakachakra"; these are auspicious symbols in Indian iconography</note> upon his hands and feet. His body emits a fragrance like the rutting-fluid of a royal elephant. He has a deep, resonant, and pleasant voice. **8**

He is handsome, highly intelligent, and a scholar of the sciences. Though he enjoys high status and luxuries, he remains private and self-controlled. He is devoted to righteous deeds, has a broad forehead, and possesses beautiful, dark, and thick hair. **9**

He is independent in all his affairs and does not tolerate being disparaged by his own kin. His wealth and resources are constantly enjoyed by others and those seeking his help. He is capable of bearing great burdens and shall become the ruler of the region of **Kanyakubja** <note>modern-day Kannauj, historically a seat of great empires</note>. Blessed with the happiness of a wife and children, this king lives for eighty years. **11**

**Thus ends the Bhadra type.**

The **Hamsa** <note>a "Great Man" yoga caused by a strong Jupiter; the word means "Swan"</note> type has a prominent nose and a beautiful voice like that of the King of the Gods. He has full cheeks and possesses great internal strength. His feet bear the marks of a <term>bow, a fish, a lotus, and a grain of barley</term>. His skin and head are beautiful and clear. **12**

He has a great fondness for lakes and water and is very passionate. He wanders to various places and his behavior toward women is respectful. He is industrious and his lifespan is equal to that of a great sage. **13**

His body is marked by the signs of a <term>termite mound, a banner, and a fish</term>. He enjoys the regions of the Ganges and Yamuna rivers. Having enjoyed the world, the Hamsa type attains a glorious death, as described by the ancient sages. **14**

**Thus ends the Hamsa type.**

<meta>This page continues the catalog of the "Five Great Men" (Pancha Mahapurusha) yogas. Having previously discussed the Mars-influenced Ruchaka and Mercury-influenced Bhadra types, the text now details the Malavya type (influenced by Venus) and concludes the entire treatise on horoscopy.</meta>

<margin>

75
</margin>

His appearance is quite striking: his body is somewhat unevenly built, not overly reddish in complexion, and slender at the waist. He possesses a dark, pleasing luster with dark tips [of the hair]. He is prone to sudden anger but is easily satisfied; his body is marked by steady waves [of muscles or energy]. **15** His knees and the crown of his head are well-formed and radiant. For many years, he remains graceful. Within the space of his ears, the fingers of his hands and feet appear dark and well-proportioned. He is the lord of the **Malavya** <note>a "Great Man" yoga caused by a strong Venus; also associated with the Malwa region</note> lineage, a ruler who ensures success and the destruction of enemies. **16**

When entering the battlefield, he is skillful. His eyes are large and beautiful like a lotus, and he is deeply attached to prosperity. He may take interest in the wealth of others, yet he remains a devoted son to his mother. He is tall, slender in the middle, and possesses a refined intellect that works for the benefit of others. **17**

The lines on his hands and feet take the forms of a <term>Mrudanga</term> <note>a traditional barrel-shaped drum</note>, a garland, and a <term>Veena</term> <note>a classical stringed instrument</note>. He also bears signs like the <term>Vajra</term> <note>a thunderbolt or diamond symbol of power</note> and a stone. Such a man performs the deeds of a king. He is described by the great sages as a lord of the earth, marked by the sign of a conch. **18**

Delighting like the moon, even if he is surrounded by worldly things, he remains high-souled. If one is endowed with these qualities, they possess the true arts of noble existence. **19**

Thus ends the description of the **Five Great Men** in the **Ornament of Nativity** <note>original: "Jatakabharane"</note>. Thus the **Light of Shambhu’s Astrology** <note>original: "Shri Shambhu Hora Prakashah"</note> is fully and completely finished.
